Fig. 1:
Distribution of the error exponent of the (a) i.i.d. and (b) constant-composition codes over the BSC with
M=4
,
n=10,000
, symmetric input distribution and composition, and
p=0.11
. Histograms of
E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})
with
10^{7}
trials, dashed black lines are normal distributions, and solid blue lines are the distributions of
\min_{i\neq j}U_{ij}
.

We observe that the cumulative distribution function of the random variable in the right-hand side of (
23
) can be easily obtained using that, for any sequence of random variables of length
L>1
, for example
X_{1},\ldots,X_{L}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\min\{X_{1},X_{2},\cdots,X_{L}\}\geq t]=\mathbb{P}[X_{1}\geq t]\cdots\mathbb{P}[X_{L}\geq t].

(24)

If such sequence is i.i.d. standard-normally distributed, then

\mathbb{P}[\min\{X_{1},X_{2},\cdots,X_{L}\}\geq t]=Q(t)^{L},

(25)

where
Q(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-x^{2}/2}{\rm d}x
is the Gaussian tail function. It is obvious that the right-hand side of (
25
) does
not
correspond to a Gaussian cumulative distribution function.

Theorem 6

Let
M_{n}
be a subexponential number of messages, namely
\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\log M_{n}=0
, satisfying the condition

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}<\infty.

(26)

Then, the error exponent of codes in the i.i.d. and constant-composition random-coding ensembles satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var(E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

(27)

Proof:

Sec.
V-F
.
∎

The proof of Theorem
6
is based on the fact that, for both i.i.d. and constant-composition, the error exponent
E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})
is the minimum of an infinite number of terms, where each term converges to a Gaussian distribution.

For a constant number of messages
M_{n}=M
, the condition in Theorem
6
is not satisfied, and therefore the error exponent does not concentrate according to (
27
) but to (
23
) instead. The fact that
M_{n}
does not grow with
n
implies that the dependence between the codewords vanishes as
n\to\infty
, and therefore the independence of
U_{ij}
is preserved. On the contrary, for a (sub-exponentially) growing number of messages
M_{n}
, the dependence among the codewords, and therefore the correlation among
U_{ij}
, increases such that the random variables
U_{ij}
can be represented by a common Gaussian random variable
U
. One example of sub-exponential growth of the number of messages satisfying (
26
) is the polynomial function of
n
given, for some
\delta>0
, by
M_{n}=\Omega\bigl(n^{\frac{1+\delta}{2}}\bigr)
.

IV
General Channels

In this section, we introduce a number of results related to the concentration of the error probability and error exponent for relatively general channels and ensembles.
The first result is a direct consequence of elementary probability results such as Chebyshev’s inequality or Jensen’s inequality.

Theorem 7

For a general channel and general ensemble such that
\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\to 0
for
0\leq R<C
and
\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\to 1
for
R>C
, we have

\displaystyle P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm p})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}0.

(28)

Proof:

Sec.
V-G
.
∎

The above result holds for general channels and general ensembles as long as the strong converse property is satisfied. For channels and ensembles only satisfying a weak converse, namely that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]>\epsilon
, then (
28
) is valid if
\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})^{2}]/\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]^{2}\to 1
as
n\to\infty
. In fact, such condition also guarantees the convergence in probability of the error exponent
E_{n}({\cal C}_{n})
to the TRC, obtained by a direct application of Markov’s inequality for general channels and pairwise-independent ensembles. Since the next results are very general and only assume pairwise-independent codewords, we drop the single-letter input distribution
Q
in the notation of
E_{\rm trc}(R)
and
E_{\rm rce}(R)
for the rest of the section.

Theorem 8

For a general channel and a pairwise-independent ensemble, under the condition that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]^{2}}\to 1,

(29)

we have

\displaystyle E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm p})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}E_{\rm trc}(R).

(30)

Proof:

Sec.
V-H
.
∎

As a remark, the condition (
29
) might hold for all the rate less than or equal to the critical rate, but not too small. This idea is made more precise in the following result, based on
[
15
, Th. 1]
and the Paley-Zygmund inequality
[
26
]
, valid for general channels and pairwise-independent ensembles.

Theorem 9

For a general channel and pariwise-independent ensemble with rate such that
E_{\rm trc}(R)>E_{\rm rce}(R)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})^{2}]}\to 0.

(31)

Proof:

Sec.
V-I
.
∎

In words, at low rates where the typical random-coding error exponent is strictly larger than the random-coding error exponent, the second-order moment of the error probability vanishes slower than the squared first-order moment. This implies that
\var\left(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\right)
vanishes slower than the squared ensemble average
\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]^{2}
, suggesting that the error probability cannot converge to a Gaussian distribution this rate regime. Such intuition is formalized in the next result, based on Theorem
9
and Slutsky’s theorem
[
23
]
.

Theorem 10

For any code ensemble and channel such that
E_{\rm trc}(R)>E_{\rm rce}(R)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.875pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}\,}}}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

(32)

Proof:

Sec.
V-J
.
∎

Theorem 11

For a general channel and pairwise-independent ensemble such that the normalized error probability converges in distribution to the standard normal distribution, that is

\displaystyle\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}\mathcal{N}(0,1),

(33)

we have that

\displaystyle E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm p})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}E_{\rm trc}(R).

(34)

Proof:

Sec.
V-K
.
∎

We remark that condition (
33
) is sufficient, but not necessary. To show (
34
), we bound the tail probabilities of the error probability
P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})
around
2^{-nE_{\rm trc}(R)}
and relate such bounds with the standard normal distribution.

In the remaining of the section, we state two auxiliary results related to the convergence in distribution of the union bound to the error probability of a code
\mathscr{c}_{n}
in (
20
), and the convergence in distribution of an arbitrary function of the error probability.

By applying
[
25
, Th. 3.6]
, a result for the sum of random variables with local dependence, to the union bound
20
, we obtain the following result.

Theorem 12

Let
Y_{12}
and
\gamma^{2}
be two parameters respectively given by
Y_{12}=\mathbb{P}\big[\{\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2}\}\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{P}[\{\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2}\}]\big]
and
\gamma^{2}=\var\bigl(\mathbb{P}[\{\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2}\}]\bigr)
. For general channels and i.i.d. ensembles such that

\displaystyle\frac{M_{n}}{\gamma^{3}}\mathbb{E}[|Y_{12}|^{3}]\to 0

(35)

\displaystyle\frac{M_{n}}{\gamma^{4}}\mathbb{E}[|Y_{12}|^{4}]\to 0

(36)

as
n\to\infty
, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

(37)

Proof:

Sec.
V-L
.
∎

Despite the result is about an upper bound on the error probability, Theorem
12
gives sufficient conditions for convergence in probability of the probability of error, while Theorem
10
gives a sufficient condition that prevents this to happen. This implies that for all codes and channels such that the two conditions (
35
), (
36
) hold, the condition
E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)>E_{\rm rce}(R,Q)
cannot be satisfied.

To obtain a similar result for the exact error probability
P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})
, that is a sum of all-dependent random variables, we note that a simple application of Hölder’s inequality suggests that
[
25
, Th. 3.6]
is too loose. While other Stein method-based approaches such as exchangeable pairs could be applied, it is actually very challenging to find a partner for the error probability. In addition, the error exponent
E_{n}({\cal C}_{n})
is
not
even a sum of random variables.

In the last result, we develop a general condition for the convergence in distribution of a random variable sequence to the standard normal random variable based on Stein’s method. Our method is based on a modification of the Wasserstein metric as in the proof of Theorem
6
, and requires channels and decoding rules to satisfy certain conditions. It is open to find which specific channels and (random) codebook ensembles such that these conditions hold.

Theorem 13

Let
g_{n}:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
be an arbitrary sequence of functions. For general channels and random codebook ensembles, under the condition that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|\frac{g_{n}(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))]}{\sqrt{\var(g_{n}(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})))}}\bigg|\bigg]\to 0,

(38)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|\bigg(\frac{g_{n}(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))]}{\sqrt{\var(g_{n}(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})))}}\bigg)^{2}-1\bigg|\bigg]\to 0,

(39)

the following holds:

\displaystyle\frac{g_{n}(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))]}{\sqrt{\var(g_{n}(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})))}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

(40)

Proof:

Sec.
V-M
.
∎Summarizing, in Sec.
III
and Sec.
IV
we discussed a number of convergence results of the error probability
P_{\rm e}({\cal C}_{n})
and the error exponent
E_{n}({\cal C}_{n})
, important performance metrics of random codes used in the study of reliable communication. In the next section, we present the proofs for such results.

V
Proofs of Theorems

We begin by introducing some definitions used in the Stein’s method
[
25
]
. We also introduce a novel, modified Wasserstein metric that is used throughout the section.

Definition 1

Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}=\bigg\{h:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}:h(u)=\begin{cases}c,&x\leq a\\
0,&x\geq a+c\\
\mbox{linear between $a$ and $a+c$},&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},\quad\mbox{for some}\quad c>0,a\in\mathbb{R}\bigg\}.

(41)

Definition 2
(Probability metrics)

For two random variables
X
and
Y
, the probability metrics have the following form:

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{H}}(X,Y)=\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\big|\mathbb{E}[h(X)]-\mathbb{E}[h(Y)]\big|,

(42)

\displaystyle\bar{d}_{\mathcal{H}}(X,Y)=\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\min\bigg\{\big|\mathbb{E}[h(X)]-\mathbb{E}[h(Y)]\big|,\big|\mathbb{E}[h(-X)]-\mathbb{E}[h(Y)]\big|\bigg\},

(43)

where
\mathcal{H}
is some family of “test” functions on
\mathbb{R}
.

We now details examples of metrics of this form along with some useful properties and relations:

•

By taking
\mathcal{H}=\{\mathbf{1}\{\cdot\leq u\}\>u\in\mathbb{R}\}
in (
43
) and the probability metric
d_{\mathcal{H}}(X,Y)
, we obtain the Kolmogorov metric, which denote by
d_{K}
. By definition, the convergence in the Kolmogorov metric means the convergence in distribution.

•

By taking
\mathcal{H}=\{h:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}:|h(u)-h(v)|\leq|u-v|\}
and the probability metric
d_{\mathcal{H}}(X,Y)
, we obtain the Wasserstein metric, which we denote
d_{W}
.

•

By taking
\mathcal{H}=\{h\in\mathcal{V}:c\leq 4\sqrt{2\pi}\}
and the probability metric
d_{\mathcal{H}}(X,Y)
, we obtain a slightly modified Wasserstein metric
\tilde{d}_{W,\rm{mod}}
.

•

By taking
\mathcal{H}=\{h\in\mathcal{V}:c\leq 4\sqrt{2\pi}\}
and the probability metric
\bar{d}_{\mathcal{H}}(X,Y)
, we obtain a modified Wasserstein metric
1
1

1

This definition of Wasserstein metric is a variant of the definition in
[
25
]
, where we constraint the set
\mathcal{H}
to achieve a tighter bound.
, which we denote
d_{W,\rm{mod}}
.

The following auxiliary lemma is also very important in deriving the convergence in distributions in most of lemmas and theorems in this paper, whose proof can be found in the Appendix
A-A
.

Lemma 1

Assume that
U_{n}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}U
as
n\to\infty
for some random variable
U
, and
\mathbb{E}[U_{n}]=0
and
\var(U_{n})=1
. Then, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[U]=0,\qquad\var(U)=1.

(44)

Proof:

Appendix
A-A
. ∎

V-A

Proof of Theorem
1

We start by stating De Caen’s inequality.

Lemma 2
(De Caen
[
27
]
)

Let
\{A_{i}\}_{i\in\mathcal{I}}
be finite family of events in a probability space
(\Omega,\mathbb{P})
. Then
2
2

2

We make the convention
\frac{0}{0}=0
, so that events of probability zero are not counted in (
45
).

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\bigcup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}A_{i}\bigg)\geq\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{\mathbb{P}^{2}(A_{i})}{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbb{P}[A_{i}\cap A_{j}]}

(45)

Lemma 3

Under the condition that
E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)=E_{\rm rce}(R,Q)
and for
\lambda>0
, the following holds for i.i.d. and constant-composition ensembles.

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},Q)^{\frac{\lambda}{n}}\big]=2^{-\lambda E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)}.

(46)

Proof:

Appendix
A-B
.
∎

V-A
1
Proof of Theorem
1
for
E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)=E_{\rm rce}(R,Q)

Let

\displaystyle\varphi(\lambda)=2^{-\lambda E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)}

(47)

for all
\lambda>0
, which is the Laplace transform of the constant random variable
-E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)
. Let

\varphi_{n}(\lambda)
be the Laplace transform of the distribution of
\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},Q)
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[2^{\lambda\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},Q)}\bigg]

(48)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},Q)^{\frac{\lambda}{n}}\big].

(49)

Then, by Lemma
3
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\varphi_{n}(\lambda)=2^{-\lambda E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)}.

(50)

Applying the Levy’s continuity theorem
[
20
, Sec. XIII.1]
, we obtain from (
50
) that

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},Q)\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}E_{\rm trc}(R,Q).

(51)

However, we know that the convergence in distribution to a constant implies convergence in probability, i. e.

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},Q)\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm p})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}E_{\rm trc}(R,Q).

(52)

V-A
2
Proof of Theorem
1
for
E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)>E_{\rm rce}(R,Q)

This section is devoted to the proof (
13
) for the range of rates for which
E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)>E_{\rm rce}(R,Q)
. We first need some definitions and lemmas. For this range of rates, the proof uses the union bound to the error probability (
1
) and shows that it gives a good estimate of the probability of error. The union bound is given by,

P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathscr{c}_{n})\leq P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathscr{c}_{n}),

(53)

where
P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathscr{c}_{n})
is defined in (
20
), and we define its finite-length error exponent as

E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathscr{c}_{n})=-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathscr{c}_{n}).

(54)

We denote by
E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)
and
E_{\rm rce}(R,Q)
respectively the typical error and the random coding error exponents for the fixed underlying distribution
Q
, and we define

\displaystyle d_{\rm B}(x,x^{\prime})=-\log\bigg(\sum_{y}\sqrt{W(y|x)W(y|x^{\prime})}\bigg)

(55)

to be the Bhattacharyya distance between symbols
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}
.

We assume that the DMC is such that

\displaystyle 0<D_{\mathrm{b}}=\max_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\rm B}(x,x^{\prime})<\infty,

(56)

that is, we leave the cases where
W(y|x)W(y|x^{\prime})=0
for for some
x
and
x^{\prime}
and all
y
beyond the scope of the paper. This case would correspond to a positive zero-error capacity, where some symbols cannot be confused at the decoder.

First, we introduce some auxiliary results about the exponential decay f the pairwise error probability between two codewords, using the method of types. We let
\mathcal{P}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
be the set of all joint types on
\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}
, and
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
be the set of all possible probability distributions on
\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}
.

Lemma 4

For
R<R_{\rm{crit}}
, the pairwise codeword error probability between two codewords
\bm{x}_{i},\bm{x}_{j}
given their joint type
P_{XX^{\prime}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\bm{x}_{i}\to\bm{x}_{j}|P_{XX^{\prime}}]=g_{n}(P_{XX^{\prime}})\doteq 2^{-n\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\rm B}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})}

(57)

for some function
g_{n}:\mathcal{P}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})\to[0,1]
.

Proof:

Appendix
A-C
∎

Lemma 5

Given an i.i.d. random codebook ensemble. For each
P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
, let
\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})
be the number of codeword pairs in a specific code such that their joint type is
P_{XX^{\prime}}
. Let
Q_{X}=Q_{X}^{\prime}=Q
. Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{n}=\bigg\{\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})=0,\quad\forall P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}):D(P_{XX^{\prime}}\|Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})>2R\bigg\}

(58)

which is the event that the (random) number of pairs
(i,j)\in[M_{n}]\times[M_{n}]
such that
i\neq j
and
(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(P_{XX^{\prime}})
is equal to zero for each
n
-joint type
P_{XX^{\prime}}
with
D(P_{XX^{\prime}}\|Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})>2R
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[\mathcal{V}_{n}^{c}\big]\leq 2^{-n\alpha(R)}

(59)

for some
\alpha(R)>0
for all
R\geq 0
.

Proof:

Appendix
A-D
∎

Lemma 6

Assume that
R>0
. Take an arbitrary
\nu\geq 0
such that
\nu\leq 2R
. Let

Q_{X}=Q_{X^{\prime}}=Q
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}=\bigg\{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}):D(P_{XX^{\prime}}\|Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})\leq 2R-\nu\bigg\}

(60)

and

\displaystyle D_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M}\sum_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})g_{n}(P_{XX^{\prime}}),

(61)

\displaystyle\bar{E}_{\rm{trc}}(\nu,R,Q)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}R+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}(x,x^{\prime}),\qquad\mbox{if}\qquad D(P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}\|Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})=2R-\nu,\\
E_{\rm rce}(R,Q),\qquad\mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}.

(62)

where
P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}
is an optimizer of
\min_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}}D(P_{XX^{\prime}}\|Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})-R
. Then,
the following holds:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[D_{n}]\doteq 2^{-n\bar{E}_{\rm{trc}}(\nu,R)}

(63)

and

\displaystyle\frac{\var(D_{n})}{\big(\mathbb{E}[D_{n}]\big)^{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle.}}{{\leq}}2^{-n\zeta(\nu,R)}

(64)

for some constant
\zeta(\nu,R)
such that
\zeta(\nu,R)>0
if
0<\nu\leq 2R
.

Proof:

Appendix
A-E
∎

Lemma 7

Let

\displaystyle E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)=\lim_{n\to\infty}-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}[\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})].

(65)

Then, for
0<R<R_{\rm{crit}}(Q)
, the following holds:

\displaystyle E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)

\displaystyle=\min_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}):D(P_{XX^{\prime}}\|Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})\leq 2R}D(P_{XX^{\prime}}\|Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})-R

(66)

\displaystyle=\bar{E}_{\rm{trc}}(0,R,Q),

(67)

where
\bar{E}_{\rm{trc}}
is defined in (
62
), Lemma
6
.

Proof:

Appendix
A-F
∎

Lemma 8

For the range of rates
0\leq R<R_{\rm{crit}}(Q)
, any
\varepsilon>0
and for some
\kappa>0
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>\frac{1}{2}2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)-\varepsilon)}\bigg]+\mathbb{P}\Big[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+\varepsilon)}\Big]\leq\frac{1}{n^{1+\kappa}}.

(68)

Proof:

Appendix
A-G
∎

Lemma 9

For all rate
R
such that
0<R<R_{\rm{crit}}(Q)
and for some
\delta(R)>0
, it holds that

\displaystyle 0\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}-1\leq 2^{-n\big(\delta(R)+E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)-E_{\rm rce}(R,Q)\big)}.

(69)

Proof:

Appendix
A-H
∎

We are now equipped to prove Theorem
1
by observing that for any
\varepsilon>0
, the convergence in probability of
E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})
to
E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)
can be written and upper bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[|E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-E_{\rm trc}(R)|>3\varepsilon\big]

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\Big[\big|E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big|>\varepsilon\Big]}_{\alpha_{n}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\left(-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big]\right)\bigg|>\varepsilon\bigg]}_{\beta_{n}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|\left(-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big]\right)-E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)\bigg|>\varepsilon\bigg]}_{\gamma_{n}}.

(70)

We next show that the terms
\alpha_{n}
,
\beta_{n}
and
\gamma_{n}
in (
70
) tend to zero as
n\to\infty
, implying the concentration result in (
13
).

V-A
3
First term of (
70
)

The term
\alpha_{n}
quantifies the deviation of the error exponent of the error probability (
2
) with that of the union bound (
20
). By the symmetry of the pairwise-independent i.i.d. random-coding ensemble, for any pair of codewords
\bm{X}_{i}
and
\bm{X}_{j}
with
i\neq j
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{P}[\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j}]\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{P}[\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2}]\big].

(71)

Similarly, for any triplet of codewords
\bm{X}_{i}
,
\bm{X}_{j}
and
\bm{X}_{k}
with
j,k\neq i
and
j\neq k
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\Big[\mathbb{P}\big[\{\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j}\}\cap\{\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{k}\}\big]\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{P}\big[\{\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2}\}\cap\{\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{3}\}\big]\Big]

(72)

where in both (
71
) and (
72
), the expectations are calculated with respect to the i.i.d. ensemble codeword distribution
Q^{n}(\bm{x})=\prod_{k=1}^{n}Q(x_{k})
, where
Q(x)
is the single-letter input distribution. We next provide separate convergence of
\alpha_{n}
for
R=0
and for
0<R<R_{\rm crit}(Q)
.

For the case of
R=0
, we first observe that the union bound (
20
) can be bounded from above as

\displaystyle P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{M_{n}}\sum_{j\neq i}\mathbb{P}[\bm{x}_{i}\to\bm{x}_{j}]

(73)

\displaystyle\leq(M_{n}-1)\max_{i\neq j}\mathbb{P}[\bm{x}_{i}\to\bm{x}_{j}],

(74)

while the probability of error (
1
) can be lower bounded by

\displaystyle P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{M_{n}}\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigcup_{j\neq i}\{\bm{x}_{i}\to\bm{x}_{j}\}\bigg]

(75)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{M_{n}}\max_{i\neq j}\mathbb{P}[\bm{x}_{i}\to\bm{x}_{j}].

(76)

From (
74
) and (
76
), we have that the first term in the r.h.s. of (
70
) satisfies

\displaystyle\alpha_{n}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>2^{n\varepsilon}P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\bigg]

(77)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[(M_{n}-1)\max_{i\neq j}\mathbb{P}[\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j}]>2^{n\varepsilon}\frac{1}{M_{n}}\max_{i\neq j}\mathbb{P}[\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j}]\bigg]

(78)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[(M_{n}-1)>2^{n\varepsilon}\frac{1}{M_{n}}\bigg].

(79)

Since
M_{n}
is any sub-exponential sequence in
n
, the probability in (
79
) vanishes as
n\to\infty
for
\varepsilon>0
.

We now consider the case of
0<R<R_{\rm crit}(Q)
. We define the sequence
a_{n}
as

\displaystyle a_{n}=2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+\frac{\varepsilon}{2})}.

(80)

Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\bigg|>\varepsilon\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>2^{\varepsilon n}P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\bigg]

(81)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-a_{n}-2^{\varepsilon n}\big(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-a_{n}\big)>(2^{\varepsilon n}-1)a_{n}\bigg]

(82)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-a_{n}>\frac{1}{2}(2^{\varepsilon n}-1)a_{n}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{P}\bigg[-2^{\varepsilon n}\big(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-a_{n}\big)>\frac{1}{2}(2^{\varepsilon n}-1)a_{n}\bigg],

(83)

where (
83
) follows from the fact that
\mathbb{P}[A+B>2C]=\mathbb{P}[\{A>C\}\cup\{B>C\}]\leq\mathbb{P}[A>C]+\mathbb{P}[B>C]
.

Now, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-a_{n}>\frac{1}{2}(2^{\varepsilon n}-1)a_{n}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>\frac{1}{2}(2^{\varepsilon n}+1)a_{n}\bigg]

(84)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>\frac{1}{2}(2^{\varepsilon n}+1)2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+\varepsilon/2)}\bigg]

(85)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>\frac{1}{2}2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)-\varepsilon/2)}\bigg].

(86)

On the other hand, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[-2^{\varepsilon n}\big(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-a_{n}\big)>\frac{1}{2}(2^{\varepsilon n}-1)a_{n}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\bigg[2^{\varepsilon n}(P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))-2^{\varepsilon n}\bigg(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-a_{n}\bigg)>\frac{1}{2}(2^{\varepsilon n}-1)a_{n}\bigg]

(87)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[2^{\varepsilon n}(P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))>\frac{1}{4}(2^{\varepsilon n}-1)a_{n}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\mathbb{P}\bigg[-2^{\varepsilon n}\bigg(P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-a_{n}\bigg)>\frac{1}{4}(2^{\varepsilon n}-1)a_{n}\bigg].

(88)

Now, we know that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[-2^{\varepsilon n}\bigg(P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-a_{n}\bigg)>\frac{1}{4}(2^{\varepsilon n}-1)a_{n}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<\bigg(1-\frac{1}{4}\bigg(\frac{2^{\varepsilon n}-1}{2^{\varepsilon n}}\bigg)\bigg)a_{n}\bigg]

(89)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<\bigg(1-\frac{1}{4}\bigg(\frac{2^{\varepsilon n}-1}{2^{\varepsilon n}}\bigg)\bigg)2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+\varepsilon/2)}\bigg]

(90)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+\varepsilon/2)}\bigg].

(91)

In addition, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[2^{\varepsilon n}(P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))>\frac{1}{4}(2^{\varepsilon n}-1)a_{n}\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle.}}{{\leq}}a_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]

(92)

\displaystyle=2^{(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+\varepsilon/2)n}\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})],

(93)

where (
92
) follows from

P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})
and Markov’s inequality, and (
93
) follows from (
80
).

Now, for
R>0
and
E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)>E_{\rm rce}(R,Q)
, from Lemma
9
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\bigg(\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}-1\bigg)

(94)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle.}}{{\leq}}2^{-nE_{\rm rce}(R,Q)}\bigg(2^{-n\big(\delta(R)+E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)-E_{\rm rce}(R,Q)\big)}\bigg).

(95)

From (
93
) and (
95
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[2^{\varepsilon n}(P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))>\frac{1}{4}(2^{\varepsilon n}-1)a_{n}\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle.}}{{\leq}}2^{\big(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+\varepsilon/2\big)n}2^{-nE_{\rm rce}(R,Q)}\bigg[2^{-n\big(\delta(R)+E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)-E_{\rm rce}(R,Q)\big)}\bigg]

(96)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle.}}{{\leq}}2^{-n\big(\delta(R)-\varepsilon/2\big)}.

(97)

Hence, from (
88
), (
91
), and (
97
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[-2^{\varepsilon n}\big(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-a_{n}\big)>\frac{1}{2}(2^{\varepsilon n}-1)a_{n}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<2^{-N(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+\varepsilon/2)}\bigg]+2^{-n\big(\delta(R)-\varepsilon/2\big)}.

(98)

From (
83
), (
86
), and (
98
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\frac{-\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{n}\bigg|>\varepsilon\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>\frac{1}{2}2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)-\varepsilon/2)}\bigg]+\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+\varepsilon/2)}\bigg]+2^{-n\big(\delta(R)-\varepsilon/2\big)}

(99)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{n^{1+\beta}}+2^{-n\big(\delta(R)-\varepsilon/2\big)}

(100)

\displaystyle\qquad\to 0,

(101)

for any
0<\varepsilon<2\delta(R)
, where (
100
) follows from Lemma
8
with
\beta
being a positive constant. Since
\mathbb{P}\big[\big|E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big|>\varepsilon\big]
is a non-increasing function in
\varepsilon
, (
101
) must hold for all
\varepsilon>0
.

Furthermore, since
\mathbb{P}\big[\big|E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big|>\varepsilon\big]
is a non-increasing function in
\varepsilon
, for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists an
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,2\delta(R))
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[\big|E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big|>\varepsilon\big]\leq\frac{1}{n^{1+\beta}}+2^{-n\big(\delta(R)-\varepsilon_{0}/2\big)}

(102)

for some
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,2\delta(R))
. It follows from (
102
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big[\big|E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big|>\varepsilon\big]<\infty.

(103)

Hence, by Borel-Cantelli’s lemma
[
23
]
, we have

\displaystyle E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\xrightarrow{(\rm a.s.)}0,

(104)

where
\xrightarrow{(\rm a.s.)}
denotes almost sure convergence as
n\to\infty
, that is, a sequence of random variables
\{A_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
converge almost surely to
A
if

\mathbb{P}\left[\lim_{n\to\infty}A_{n}=A\right]=1.

(105)

On the other hand, observe that

\displaystyle\big|E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big|

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{2\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{n}

(106)

\displaystyle\leq 2E_{\rm{sp}}(R),

(107)

where (
107
) follows from the fact that the error exponent of any sufficiently long code is upper bounded by the sphere-packing bound.

Hence, from (
104
) and (
107
), by the bounded convergence theorem, it holds that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big]=0.

(108)

This means that

\displaystyle E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}E_{n}(\mathcal{C}_{n})

(109)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})

(110)

\displaystyle=E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q).

(111)

V-A
4
Second term of (
70
)

Using Chebyshev’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\left(-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big]\right)\bigg|>\varepsilon\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\var\bigg(-\frac{\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{n}\bigg)

(112)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{n^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}\var\bigg(-\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\bigg)

(113)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{n^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(-\log(M_{n}-1)-\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{M_{n}-1}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\bigg]-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\bigg(\frac{\mathbb{E}\big[-\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big]}{n}\bigg)^{2}.

(114)

Now, define

\displaystyle\xi(p,n,R)=2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+R)}.

(115)

From (
114
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\left(-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big]\right)\bigg|>\varepsilon\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(-\log(M_{n}-1)-\log\xi(p,n,R)-\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\bigg(\frac{\mathbb{E}\big[-\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big]}{n}\bigg)^{2}.

(116)

By Lemma
7
, we know that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}\big[-\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big]}{n}=E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q),

(117)

hence, it holds that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\left(-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big]\right)\bigg|>\varepsilon\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad=\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)-\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\bigg]-\frac{\big(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)\big)^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}

(118)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\bigg((E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q))^{2}-2E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\bigg]\bigg)-\frac{\big(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)\big)^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}},

(119)

where (
119
) follows from the sub-additivity of
\limsup
.
Now, we need to estimate

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)\bigg]

and

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\bigg].

First, we show that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)\xrightarrow{(\rm a.s.)}0

(120)

Indeed, take an arbitrary
\nu>0
and observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)\bigg|>\nu\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)\bigg|>n\nu\bigg]

(121)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|-\log\xi(p,n,R)+\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{M_{n}-1}\bigg)\bigg|>n\nu\bigg]

(122)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|n(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+R)+\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{M_{n}-1}\bigg)\bigg|>n\nu\bigg]

(123)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{M_{n}-1}>2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+R-\nu)}\bigg]+\mathbb{P}\bigg[\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{M_{n}-1}<2^{-N(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+R+\nu)}\bigg]

(124)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>\frac{1}{2}2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)-\nu)}\bigg]+\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+\nu)}\bigg]

(125)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{n^{1+\beta}},

(126)

for some constants
\beta>0
, where (
126
) follows from Lemma
8
.

From (
126
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)\bigg|>\nu\bigg]<\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{1+\beta}}<\infty

(127)

by using D’Alembert criterion.

This means that (
120
) holds, or

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)\xrightarrow{(\rm a.s.)}0

(128)

by Borel-Cantelli lemma
[
23
]
.

Now, since
0\leq\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j})\leq 1
for all
i,j\in[M]:i\neq j
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\sum_{i\neq j}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j})\bigg)

(129)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{1}{\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)

(130)

\displaystyle\leq E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+R,

(131)

where (
131
) follows from (
115
).
On the other hand, from the sphere-packing bound
3
3

3

In case that the sphere packing bound diverges, we can use
E_{\rm ex}(R=0)
as an upper bound, which is finite at
R=0
unless the zero error capacity
C_{0}>0
.
, it holds almost surely that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)

(132)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle.}}{{\geq}}\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{2^{-nE_{\rm{sp}}(R)}}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)

(133)

\displaystyle=-E_{\rm{sp}}(R)-R+\big(E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)+R\big)

(134)

\displaystyle=E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)-E_{\rm{sp}}(R),

(135)

where (
133
) follows from the sphere-packing bound
[
28
]
,
and (
134
) follows from (
115
) and
M_{n}\doteq 2^{nR}
.

From (
131
) and (
135
),
\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)
is bounded (both below and above).

Hence, by the bounded convergence theorem and the continuous mapping theorem
[
23
]
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\to 0,

(136)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{(M_{n}-1)\xi(p,n,R)}\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\to 0.

(137)

From (
119
), (
136
), and (
137
), we finally have

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\left(-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big]\right)\bigg|>\varepsilon\bigg]=0

(138)

for any arbitrary
\varepsilon>0
. This leads to

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|E_{n}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\left(-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big]\right)\bigg|>\varepsilon\bigg]=0

(139)

by the fact that the probability measure is bounded from below by zero.

V-A
5
Third term of (
70
)

By Lemma
7
, it is known that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{-\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{n}\bigg]\to E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q).

(140)

On the other hand, from (
111
) in Step 1, we know that

\displaystyle E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)=E_{\rm trc}(R,Q).

(141)

It follows from (
140
) and (
141
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{-\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{n}\bigg]-E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)\bigg|>\varepsilon\bigg]\to 0.

(142)

In conclusion, as anticipated, the three terms of (
70
) tend to zero as
n\to\infty
, showing (
13
) for rates below the critical rate. Together with Subsection
V-A1
, we proved Theorem
1
, which states the convergence in probability of the error exponent of the codes in the ensemble to the typical random-coding error exponent.

V-A
6
Extension to Constant Composition Codes

For the constant-composition code, for all the rate
R_{\rm{crit}}(R,Q)\leq R\leq C
, the proof of Theorem
1
holds by using the Levy’s continuity theorem since it is not hard to see that
E_{\rm rce}(R,Q)=E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)
for this case. At all the rate
0\leq R\leq E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)
, Lemma
4
- Lemma
6
still hold since
\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}
and
\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{k},\bm{X}_{l})\in\mathcal{T}(\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}})\}
are still pairwise-independent for the constant-composition code for all
\{i,j,k,l\in[M]:i\neq j,k\neq l\}
. In Lemma
7
, the typical error exponent of the union bound should be replaced by
E_{\rm{trc}}^{\rm{ub}}
for the constant-composition code in
[
9
]
. To show that Theorem
1
still holds for the constant-composition code, we need to prove that the mapping from the error probability and the union bound in Lemma
8
and Lemma
9
still work. It is not hard to see that the proof of Lemma
8
still holds for the constant-composition code since its correctness depends on Lemma
4
, Lemma
6
and the fact that
\tilde{V}_{ij}
’s are pairwise-independent where
\tilde{V}_{ij}
is defined (
748
). Lemma
9
still holds for the constant-composition code, i.e.,

Lemma 10

For any constant-composition code with type
Q
and for all the rate
R
such that
0<R<R_{\rm{crit}}
, it holds that

\displaystyle 0\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}-1\leq 2^{-N\big(\delta(R)+E_{\rm trc}^{\rm ub}(R,Q)-E_{\rm rce}(R,Q)\big)}

(143)

for some
\delta(R)>0
.

Proof:

To prove Lemma
10
, we use the same proof as Lemma
9
in Appendix
A-H
. It is easy to check that (
796
) still holds for the constant-composition code. In addition, the pairwise error probability only depends on the joint-type of the two codewords as in the i.i.d .case.
∎

V-B

Proof of Theorem
2

First, we prove (
18
).
Under the condition that
E_{\rm rce}(R,Q)=E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)
, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon)}\bigg]

(144)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{n(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon)}\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]

(145)

\displaystyle\doteq 2^{n(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon)}2^{-nE_{\rm rce}(R,Q)}

(146)

\displaystyle=2^{-n\varepsilon},

(147)

where (
145
) follows from the Markov inequality, (
146
) follows from
\mathbb{E}\big[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big]\doteq 2^{-nE_{\rm rce}(R,Q)}
, (
147
) follows from
E_{\rm rce}(R,Q)=E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)
.

Now, for any
s>0
, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\frac{s}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>-s(E_{\rm trc}(R)-\varepsilon)\bigg]

(148)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[2^{\frac{s}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}>2^{-s(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon)}\bigg]

(149)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{s(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon)}\mathbb{E}\bigg[2^{\frac{s}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}\bigg]

(150)

\displaystyle=2^{s(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon)}\mathbb{E}\big[\big(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big)^{s/n}\big]

(151)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{s(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon)}\mathbb{E}\big[\big(P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big)^{s/n}\big].

(152)

On the other hand, for any
0\leq s\leq n
and
\lambda>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big(P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big)^{s/n}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{1}{M}\sum_{i\neq j}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j})\bigg)^{s/n}\bigg]

(153)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{i\neq j}\bigg(\frac{1}{M}\sum_{i\neq j}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j})\bigg)^{s/n}\bigg]

(154)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M^{s/n}}\sum_{i\neq j}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j})\bigg)^{s/n}\bigg]

(155)

\displaystyle=\frac{M(M-1)}{M^{s/n}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2})\bigg)^{s/n}\bigg]

(156)

\displaystyle\leq M^{2-\frac{s}{n}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2})\bigg)^{s/n}\bigg]

(157)

\displaystyle=M^{2-\frac{s}{n}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\big[\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2})\big]^{1+\lambda}\bigg)^{\frac{s}{n(1+\lambda)}}\bigg],

(158)

where (
154
) follows from

\displaystyle(x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{n})^{\alpha}\leq x_{1}^{\alpha}+x_{2}^{\alpha}+\cdots+x_{n}^{\alpha}

(159)

for any
x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{n}\geq 0
and
\alpha\in[0,1]
, and (
158
) follows from
0\leq\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2})\leq 1
and
\lambda>0
, so
\big(\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2})\big)^{s/n}\leq\big(\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2})\big)^{\frac{s}{n(1+\lambda)}}
.

On the other hand, by Lemma
4
, the pairwise codeword error probability
\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2})
given their joint type
Q_{XX^{\prime}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2})\doteq 2^{-n\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{1k},X_{2k})=(x,x^{\prime})\}},

(160)

where

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})=-\log\bigg(\sum_{y}\sqrt{W(y|x)W(y|x^{\prime})}\bigg).

(161)

Hence, for any
0\leq s\leq n
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2})\big)^{\frac{s}{n}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[2^{\frac{s}{n}\log\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2})}\bigg]

(162)

\displaystyle\doteq\mathbb{E}\bigg[2^{-\frac{s}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{1k},X_{2k})=(x,x^{\prime})\}}\bigg].

(163)

Now, since
\{\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{1k},X_{2k})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\}_{k=1}^{n}
are i.i.d., hence by the SLLN, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{1k},X_{2k})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\xrightarrow{(\rm a.s.)}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime}).

(164)

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle 0\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{1k},X_{2k})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\leq\max_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})<\infty.

(165)

Hence, by the bounded convergence theorem
[
23
]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[2^{-\frac{s}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{1k},X_{2k})=(x,x^{\prime})\}}\bigg]\to 2^{-s\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}.

(166)

Similarly, for any fixed constant
\lambda\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[2^{-\frac{s}{n(1+\lambda)}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{1k},X_{2k})=(x,x^{\prime})\}}\bigg]\to 2^{-\frac{s}{1+\lambda}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}.

(167)

Hence, from (
166
) and (
167
), for any fixed constant
\lambda\geq 0
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[2^{-\frac{s}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{1k},X_{2k})=(x,x^{\prime})\}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad=(1+o(1))\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[2^{-\frac{s}{n(1+\lambda)}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{1k},X_{2k})=(x,x^{\prime})\}}\bigg]\bigg)^{1+\lambda}

(168)

\displaystyle\qquad=(1+o(1))\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[2^{-\frac{s}{n(1+\lambda)}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{11},X_{21})=(x,x^{\prime})\}}\bigg]\bigg)^{n(1+\lambda)}.

(169)

From (
158
) and (
169
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big(P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big)^{s/n}\big]

\displaystyle\leq(1+o(1))M^{2-\frac{s}{n}}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[2^{-\frac{s}{n(1+\lambda)}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{11},X_{21})=(x,x^{\prime})\}}\bigg]\bigg)^{n(1+\lambda)}.

(170)

Now, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[2^{-\frac{s}{n(1+\lambda)}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{11},X_{21})=(x,x^{\prime})\}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}((X_{11},X_{21})=(x,x^{\prime}))\mathbb{E}\bigg[2^{-\frac{s}{n(1+\lambda)}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{11},X_{21})=(x,x^{\prime})\}}\bigg|(X_{11},X_{21})=(x,x^{\prime})\bigg]

(171)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{s}{n(1+\lambda)}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}.

(172)

From (
170
) and (
172
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big(P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big)^{\frac{s}{n}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq(1+o(1))M^{2-\frac{s}{n}}\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{s}{n(1+\lambda)}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}\bigg)^{n(1+\lambda)}.

(173)

From (
152
) and (
173
), for any
s
such that
0\leq s\leq n
and any fixed constant
\lambda>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\frac{s}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>-s(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon)\bigg]

(174)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\bigg[2^{\frac{s}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}>2^{-s(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon)}\bigg]

(175)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2^{s(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon)}\mathbb{E}\bigg[2^{\frac{s}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}\bigg]

(176)

\displaystyle\qquad=2^{s(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon)}\mathbb{E}\big[\big(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big)^{\frac{s}{n}}\big]

(177)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(1+o(1))2^{s(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon)}M^{2-\frac{s}{n}}\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{s}{n(1+\lambda)}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}\bigg)^{n(1+\lambda)}.

(178)

From (
178
), by choosing
s=n
and using
M=2^{nR}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq(1+o(1))2^{n\big(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)+\big(2-\frac{s}{n}\big)R-\varepsilon\big)}\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda}}\bigg)^{n(1+\lambda)}

(179)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))2^{n(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)+R-\varepsilon)}2^{n(1+\lambda)\log\big(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda}}\big)}

(180)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))2^{n\big[E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)+R+(1+\lambda)\log\big(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda}}\big)\big]}2^{-n\varepsilon}.

(181)

Now, for
E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)\neq E_{\rm rce}(R,Q)
, from (
111
) and Lemma
7
, observe that

\displaystyle E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)=\min_{P_{XX^{\prime}}:D(P_{XX^{\prime}}\|Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})\leq 2R}D(P_{XX^{\prime}}\|Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})-R.

(182)

Given the distribution
Q
and
Q_{X}=Q_{X^{\prime}}=Q
, the optimization problem in (
182
) is convex in
\{P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})\}_{x,x^{\prime}}
since the KL divergence is convex. By using standard KKT conditions, it is easy to see that (
182
) has as optimal solution:

\displaystyle P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\{P_{XX^{\prime}}^{0},P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}\},

(183)

where

\displaystyle P_{XX^{\prime}}^{0}(x,x^{\prime})=\frac{Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}}{\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}},

(184)

and

\displaystyle P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}(x,x^{\prime})=\frac{Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda_{*}}}}{\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda_{*}}}}.

(185)

Here,
\lambda^{*}
is the unique positive solution of the following equation:

\displaystyle 2R=D(P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}(x,x^{\prime})\|Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}}).

(186)

Now, if
P_{XX^{\prime}}=P_{XX^{\prime}}^{0}
, then we have

\displaystyle E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)+R+\log\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad=D(P_{XX^{\prime}}^{0}\|Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}^{0}(x,x^{\prime})+\log\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}\bigg)

(187)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}\frac{Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}}{\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}}\log\frac{2^{-d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}}{\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}^{0}(x,x^{\prime})+\log\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}\bigg)

(188)

\displaystyle\qquad=-\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}\frac{Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}}{\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})-\log\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}^{0}(x,x^{\prime})+\log\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}\bigg)

(189)

\displaystyle\qquad=-\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}P_{XX^{\prime}}^{0}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})-\log\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}^{0}(x,x^{\prime})+\log\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}\bigg)

(190)

\displaystyle\qquad=0.

(191)

Hence, by choosing
\lambda=0
, from (
181
) and (
191
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon\bigg]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle.}}{{\leq}}2^{-n\varepsilon}.

(192)

Similarly, for the case
P_{XX^{\prime}}=P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)+R+(1+\lambda^{*})\log\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda^{*}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad=D(P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}\|Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}(x,x^{\prime})+(1+\lambda^{*})\log\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda^{*}}}\bigg)

(193)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(1+\lambda^{*})D(P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}\|Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}(x,x^{\prime})+(1+\lambda^{*})\log\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda^{*}}}\bigg)

(194)

\displaystyle\qquad=(1+\lambda^{*})\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}\frac{Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda^{*}}}}{\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda^{*}}}}\log\frac{2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda^{*}}}}{\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda^{*}}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}(x,x^{\prime})+(1+\lambda^{*})\log\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda^{*}}}\bigg)

(195)

\displaystyle\qquad=-(1+\lambda^{*})\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}\frac{Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda^{*}}}}{\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda^{*}}}}\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda^{*}}-(1+\lambda^{*})\log\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda^{*}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}(x,x^{\prime})+\log\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda^{*}}}\bigg)

(196)

\displaystyle\qquad=-\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})-(1+\lambda^{*})\log\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda^{*}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}(x,x^{\prime})+\log\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})2^{-\frac{d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})}{1+\lambda^{*}}}\bigg)

(197)

\displaystyle\qquad=0.

(198)

From (
181
),(
191
), and (
198
), where we set
\lambda=0
for the first case and
\lambda=\lambda^{*}
for the second one, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)-\varepsilon\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq(1+o(1))2^{-n\varepsilon}

(199)

\displaystyle\doteq 2^{-n\varepsilon}.

(200)

Finally, from (
147
) and (
200
), we obtain (
18
). This concludes our proof of (
18
) for the i.i.d. random codebook ensemble.

For the constant-composition codebook ensemble, to prove (
18
), we first prove the following lemma, which is somewhat similar to Lemma
16
.

Lemma 11

Let
X_{1},X_{2},\cdots,X_{n}
be Bernoulli random variables on
\mathbb{R}
. In addition, there exists a set
\mathcal{V}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
with cardinality
|\mathcal{V}|
such that for all
x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{n}\in\mathcal{V}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[X_{1}=x_{1},X_{2}=x_{2},\cdots,X_{n}=x_{n}]\leq\big(1+o(1)\big)\prod_{k=1}^{n}P(x_{k})

(201)

for some distribution
P
on
\{0,1\}
, and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[X_{1}=x_{1},X_{2}=x_{2},\cdots,X_{n}=x_{n}]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-n\zeta},\quad\forall(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathcal{V}^{c}

(202)

for some
\zeta>0
.
In addition,

\displaystyle 2^{-n\zeta}|\mathcal{V}^{c}|\to 0.

(203)

Let
S_{n}=X_{1}+X_{2}+\cdots+X_{n}
. Then, for any
t>0
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\prod_{i=1}^{n}P(x_{i})}\big[2^{-tS_{n}/n}\big]+2^{-n\zeta}|\mathcal{V}^{c}|.

(204)

Proof:

Appendix
A-I
.
∎It is known that
\{\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2})\in\mathcal{T}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}\}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}
satisfies all the conditions of Lemma
11
. Hence, for any
0\leq s\leq n
, from (
163
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2})\bigg)^{\frac{s}{n}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}\bigg[2^{\frac{s}{n}\log\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{1}\to\bm{X}_{2})}\bigg]

(205)

\displaystyle\qquad\doteq\mathbb{E}\bigg[2^{-\frac{s}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{1k},X_{2k})=(x,x^{\prime})\}}\bigg]

(206)

\displaystyle\qquad\doteq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2})\in\mathcal{T}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}\kern 4.09723pt\mbox{is independent}}\bigg[2^{-s\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2})\in\mathcal{T}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}}\bigg]+2^{-n\zeta}|\mathcal{V}^{c}|.

(207)

The rest follows the same as the proof of (
18
) for the i.i.d. random codebook ensemble.

Now, we prove (
19
).
For any i.i.d. and constant-composition random codebooks, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq\max_{i\neq j}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j}).

(208)

Recall the definition of
V_{n}
in (
404
). It follows from (
208
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)+\varepsilon\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[-\frac{1}{n}\log\max_{i\neq j}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j})>E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)+\varepsilon\bigg]

(209)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\bigg[V_{n}>n\big(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)+\varepsilon\big)\bigg]

(210)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\frac{V_{n}-\mathbb{E}[V_{n}]}{\sqrt{\var(V_{n})}}>\frac{n\big(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)+\varepsilon-\frac{\mathbb{E}[V_{n}]}{n}\big)}{\sqrt{\var(V_{n})}}\bigg]

(211)

\displaystyle\qquad=Q\bigg(\frac{n\big(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)+\varepsilon-\frac{\mathbb{E}[V_{n}]}{n}\big)}{\sqrt{\var(V_{n})}}\bigg)+O\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg)

(212)

\displaystyle\qquad=Q\Bigg(\frac{\sqrt{n}\big(E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)+\varepsilon-E_{\rm{trc}}(Q,0)\big)}{\sqrt{\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}^{2}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})-\big(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})\big)^{2}}}\Bigg)+O\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg)

(213)

as
n\to\infty
since
E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)\geq E_{\rm{trc}}(Q,0)
, where (
212
) follows from Theorem
6
, and (
213
) follows from (
532
) and the Berry–Esseen theorem
[
23
]
.

V-C

Proof of Theorem
3

We start with some accessory results, then prove the main part of Theorem
3
.

In the lemma below we use the simplified notation:
\mathcal{I}\{i,j\}=\mathcal{I}\{(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{x}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(P_{XX^{\prime}})\}
,
\mathcal{I}\{.\}
being the indicator function, i.e., the two considered codewords have joint type
P_{XX^{\prime}}
.

Lemma 12

\displaystyle 2^{-n2D(P_{XX^{\prime}}||Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})}\leq\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{I}\{i,j\}\mathcal{I}\{i,k\}]\leq 2^{-n[D(P_{XX^{\prime}}||Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})+\eta]}

(214)

where
0\leq\eta\leq D(P_{XX^{\prime}}||Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})
.

Proof:

Appendix
A-J
.
∎

Lemma 13

For any
\epsilon>0
and for any joint type
P_{XX^{\prime}}
such that
D(P_{XX^{\prime}}||Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})\leq R-\epsilon
,
\forall\epsilon>0
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})\leq 2^{-n\epsilon}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})]\right]\dot{\leq}2^{-2^{n\epsilon}}

(215)

Proof:

Appendix
A-K
.
∎Using Lemma
12
and Lemma
13
we prove the following theorem, which states that the probability of finding a code for which the exponent of
P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})
is larger than the expurgated exponent
E_{\rm ex}(R)
is double exponentially decaying in
n
.

Now we can prove the main part of theorem
3
. We have that

\displaystyle P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})

\displaystyle\dot{=}\max_{P_{XX^{\prime}}}\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})e^{-n[\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})+R]}

(216)

Let us refer to the maximizing joint type of (
216
) as
P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}
. We define the following complementary events:

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\{P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}\in\mathcal{P}\}

(217)

\displaystyle\overline{A}

\displaystyle=\{P_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}\}

(218)

where

\mathcal{P}=\{P_{XX^{\prime}}|D(P_{XX^{\prime}}\|Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})\leq 2R\}

while

\overline{\mathcal{P}}=\{P_{XX^{\prime}}|D(P_{XX^{\prime}}\|Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})>2R\},

Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime}
being the theoretical joint type.
Consider a positive real number
E_{2}>E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)
. We have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq E_{2}\right]=\mathbb{P}\left[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq E_{2},A\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq E_{2},\overline{A}\right]

(219)

Now we proceed to bound from above both terms at the right hand side of (
219
).

V-C
1
First Term

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq E_{2},A\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\leq 2^{-nE_{2}},A\right]

(220)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{1}{M_{n}}\sum_{P_{XX^{\prime}}}\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})2^{-n\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})}\leq 2^{-nE_{2}},A\right]

(221)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{P_{XX^{\prime}}}\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})e^{-n\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})}\leq 2^{-n(E_{2}-R)},A\right]

(222)

\displaystyle\dot{=}\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{P_{XX^{\prime}}}\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})2^{-n\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})}\leq 2^{-n(E_{2}-R)},A\right]

(223)

\displaystyle{=}\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})2^{-n\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})}\leq 2^{-n(E_{2}-R)},A\right]

(224)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})2^{-n\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})}\leq 2^{-n(E_{2}-R)}\right]

(225)

\displaystyle{=}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}}\left[\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})e^{-n\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})}\leq 2^{-n(E_{2}-R)}\right]\right\}

(226)

\displaystyle{=}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}}\left[\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})\leq 2^{-n(E_{2}-R-\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime}))}\right]\right\}

(227)

where (
224
) comes from the definition of
A
while (
225
) comes from removing the event
A
.
Let us now define
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=\{P_{XX^{\prime}}|D(P_{XX^{\prime}}\|Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})\leq R\},

(228)

and note that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{P}
. Let us consider the term
2^{-n(E_{2}-R-\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime}))}
.
We now look for a
P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
such that this is smaller than the mean of the enumerator function, i.e, a
P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
such that the following holds:

\displaystyle 2^{-n(E_{2}-R-\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime}))}\leq 2^{n[2R-D(P_{XX^{\prime}}||Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})-\epsilon]}

(229)

\displaystyle E_{2}-R-\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})\geq-[2R-D(P_{XX^{\prime}}||Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})-\epsilon]

(230)

\displaystyle E_{2}-R-\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})\geq-2R+D(P_{XX^{\prime}}||Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})+\epsilon

(231)

\displaystyle E_{2}\geq-R+D(P_{XX^{\prime}}||Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})+\epsilon.

(232)

Let us indicate the
P_{XX^{\prime}}
that minimizes (
232
) with
P_{XX^{\prime}}^{\prime}
.
Minimizing the term at the right hand side of (
232
) we can set the value of
E_{2}
to:

\displaystyle E_{2}=\min_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}-R+D(P_{XX^{\prime}}||Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime}))+\epsilon.

(233)

The right hand side of (
233
) is strictly larger than
E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)
. To see this note the following:

\displaystyle\min_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}-R+D(P_{XX^{\prime}}||Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})+\epsilon

(234)

\displaystyle=\min_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}+R-2R+D(P_{XX^{\prime}}||Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})+\epsilon

(235)

\displaystyle>\min_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}}+R-2R+D(P_{XX^{\prime}}||Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})+\epsilon

(236)

\displaystyle=\min_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{GGV}}+R-2R+D(P_{XX^{\prime}}||Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})+\epsilon

(237)

\displaystyle=\min_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{GGV}}+R+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})+\epsilon

(238)

\displaystyle=E_{\rm trc}(R,Q)+\epsilon

(239)

where (
236
) follows from the fact that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{P}
, (
237
) follows from the concavity of the objective function (minimum is on the border) while (
239
) follows from the definition of
\mathcal{Z}_{GGV}
.
With this definition of
E_{2}
we ensure that for at least one joint type the conditions for applying Lemma
13
(i.e., (
229
)) hold. Using the definition in (
227
) together with the statement of Lemma
13
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq E_{2},A\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle.}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}}\left[\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})\leq 2^{-n(E_{2}-R+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime}))}\right]\right\}

(240)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle.}}{{\leq}}2^{-2^{n[R-D(P_{XX^{\prime}}^{\prime}||Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})]}}

(241)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-2^{n\epsilon^{\prime}}}

(242)

with
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
.
