2
Second Term

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq E_{2},\overline{A}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\leq 2^{-nE_{2}},\overline{A}\right]

(243)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{1}{M_{n}}\sum_{P_{XX^{\prime}}}\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})2^{n\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})}\leq 2^{-nE_{2}},\overline{A}\right]

(244)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{P_{XX^{\prime}}}\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})2^{n\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})}\leq 2^{-n(E_{2}-R)},A\right]

(245)

\displaystyle\dot{=}\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{P_{XX^{\prime}}}\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})2^{n\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})}\leq 2^{-n(E_{2}-R)},\overline{A}\right]

(246)

Consider (
246
). The event
\overline{A}
implies that the joint type maximizing the expression at the left hand side lays outside
\mathcal{P}
. This implies that any
P_{XX^{\prime}}
which lies inside
\mathcal{P}
leads to a value which is no greater than the maximum. Since this is an implication of the events within brackets, its probability is larger than or equal to the one of (
246
).Thus we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq E_{2},\overline{A}\right]

\displaystyle\dot{=}\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{P_{XX^{\prime}}}\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})2^{n\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})}\leq 2^{-n(E_{2}-R)},\overline{A}\right]

(247)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{N}(P_{XX^{\prime}})2^{n\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})}\leq 2^{-n(E_{2}-R)}\right]

(248)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-2^{n\epsilon^{\prime}}}

(249)

where (
249
) is because (
248
) has the same form as (
225
) and thus the same inequalities as for the first term hold.

Finally, we note that from (
233
) we can further state the following:

\displaystyle E_{2}

\displaystyle=\min_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}-R+D(P_{XX^{\prime}}||Q_{X}Q_{X}^{\prime})+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})+\epsilon

(250)

\displaystyle=\min_{P_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}-\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})P_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})+\epsilon

(251)

\displaystyle=E_{\rm ex}(R)+\epsilon

(252)

where (
251
) follows from the concavity of the objective function, which implies that the minimum is on the border of the region
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, and from the definition of
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
while (
252
) is found by calculating the derivative of
[
24
, Eq. (5.7.11)]
with respect to the optimization variable
\rho
and, after some change of variable, equating to zero.

V-D

Proof of Theorem
4

Now let us consider the following inequality

P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\leq M_{n}P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})

(253)

which follows from upper-bounding the probability
\mathbb{P}[\bm{x}_{i}\to\bm{x}_{j}]
in (
20
) by
\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigcup_{j\neq i}\{\bm{x}_{i}\to\bm{x}_{j}\}\bigg]
in (
1
).
From Theorem
3
and using (
253
) we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq E_{\rm ex}(R)+R+\epsilon\right]\leq 2^{-2^{n\epsilon}}

(254)

and finally

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq E_{\rm{ex}}(0)+\epsilon\right]\leq 2^{-2^{n\epsilon}}.

(255)

V-E

Proof of Theorem
5

V-E
1
i.i.d. ensemble

Observe that

\displaystyle\max_{i\neq j}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j})\leq P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{M_{n}}\sum_{j\neq i}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j})\leq M_{n}(M_{n}-1)\max_{i\neq j}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j}).

(256)

On the other hand, by Lemma
4
, the pairwise codeword error probability
\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j})
given their joint type
P_{XX^{\prime}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j})\doteq 2^{-n\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\hat{P}_{\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{j}}(x,x^{\prime})},

(257)

where
\hat{P}_{\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{j}}
is the
n
-joint type of
(\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2})
, and

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})=-\log\bigg(\sum_{y}\sqrt{W(y|x)W(y|x^{\prime})}\bigg).

(258)

Observe that

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{j}}(x,x^{\prime})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}.

(259)

It follows from (
257
) and (
259
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j})\doteq 2^{-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}}

(260)

for all
i,j\in[M_{n}],i\neq j
.
Since
M_{n}
sub-exponential in
n
, from (
256
) and (
260
), we obtain

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\xrightarrow{(\rm a.s.)}\frac{V}{n},

(261)

where

\displaystyle V=\min_{i\neq j}Z_{ij}

(262)

with

\displaystyle Z_{ij}=-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\},

(263)

for all
i,j\in[M_{n}]
and
i\neq j
.

Now, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Z_{ij}]

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbb{P}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}

(264)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbb{P}(X_{ik}=x)\mathbb{P}(X_{jk}=x^{\prime})

(265)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime}).

(266)

In addition, we have

\displaystyle\var(Z_{ij})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\var\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\bigg)

(267)

\displaystyle=n\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{i1},X_{j1})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\bigg)^{2}\bigg]-\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}\bigg)

(268)

\displaystyle=n\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}^{2}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{1i},X_{2i})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\bigg]-\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}\bigg)

(269)

\displaystyle=n\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}^{2}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbb{P}\{(X_{1i},X_{2i})=(x,x^{\prime})\}-\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}\bigg)

(270)

\displaystyle=n\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}^{2}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})-\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}\bigg).

(271)

for all
i\neq j
.

Now, define

\displaystyle T_{ij}:

\displaystyle=\frac{Z_{ij}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{ij}]}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{ij})}}

(272)

\displaystyle=\frac{Z_{ij}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{12}]}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{12})}},

(273)

where (
273
) follows from the fact that
Z_{ij}
’s are identically distributed.

Then, by CLT, it holds that

\displaystyle T_{ij}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}\mathcal{N}(0,1),\qquad\forall i\neq j.

(274)

On the other hand, for any fixed tuple
(\{\alpha_{ij}\}:i,j\in[M],i\neq j)
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\neq j}\alpha_{ij}T_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i\neq j}\alpha_{ij}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\big(Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})-\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\big)}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{12})}}

(275)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{\sum_{i\neq j}\alpha_{ij}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\big(Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})-\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\big)}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{12})}}.

(276)

Now, by the i.i.d. random codebook generation, it holds that
\{V_{k}\}_{k=1}^{n}
are i.i.d. random variables, where

\displaystyle V_{k}=\frac{\sum_{i\neq j}\alpha_{ij}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\big(Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})-\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\big)}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{12})}}.

(277)

In addition, since
(X_{i1},X_{j1})_{i\neq j}
’s are pairwise independent, we have

\displaystyle\var(V_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i\neq j}\alpha_{ij}^{2}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}^{2}(x,x^{\prime})\var\big(\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}-Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})\big))}{\var(Z_{12})}

(278)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i\neq j}\alpha_{ij}^{2}\big[\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}^{2}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})-\big(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})\big)^{2}\big]}{n\big[\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}^{2}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})-\big(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})\big)^{2}\big]}

(279)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i\neq j}\alpha_{ij}^{2}}{n}.

(280)

Hence, it holds from (
276
) and (
280
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\neq j}\alpha_{ij}T_{ij}=\sqrt{\sum_{i\neq j}\alpha_{ij}^{2}}\bigg(\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{n}V_{k}}{\sqrt{n\var(V_{1})}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}\mathcal{N}\bigg(0,\sum_{i\neq j}\alpha_{ij}^{2}\bigg),

(281)

where (
281
) follows from the CLT. Hence, the distribution of the vector
\{T_{ij}:i,j\in[M],i\neq j\}
goes to the distribution of a jointly Gaussian random vector by the Levy’s continuity theorem
[
23
]
.

Now, it is known that the distribution of any Gaussian random vector (both p.d.f and c.d.f.) is defined by its mean and covariance matrix. Since the covariance matrix of the vector
\{T_{ij}:i,j\in[M],i\neq j\}
is the identity matrix by the pairwise independence of
T_{ij}
, which originates from the pairwise independence of
\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j})^{\prime}s
, hence, the limit distribution is the standard normal Gaussian vector with dimension
M(M-1)
. This distribution is equal to the joint distribution of
M(M-1)
independent standard normal variables
\{U_{ij}\}_{i\neq j}
. Hence, by the continuous mapping theorem
[
23
]
, it follows that

\displaystyle\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}\min_{i\neq j}U_{ij}.

(282)

Now, let

\displaystyle T=\frac{V-\mathbb{E}[V]}{\sqrt{\var(V)}},

(283)

then, we have

\displaystyle T=\min_{i\neq j}\tilde{T}_{ij},

(284)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{ij}=\frac{Z_{ij}-\mathbb{E}[V]}{\sqrt{\var(V)}},

(285)

Hence, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle=\min_{i\neq j}\frac{\sqrt{\var(Z_{ij})}}{\sqrt{\var(V)}}\tilde{T}_{ij}+\frac{\mathbb{E}[Z_{ij}]-\mathbb{E}[V]}{\sqrt{\var(V)}}

(286)

\displaystyle=\min_{i\neq j}\frac{\sqrt{\var(Z_{12})}}{\sqrt{\var(V)}}\tilde{T}_{ij}+\frac{\mathbb{E}[Z_{22}]-\mathbb{E}[V]}{\sqrt{\var(V)}}

(287)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{\var(Z_{12})}}{\sqrt{\var(V)}}\min_{i\neq j}\tilde{T}_{ij}+\frac{\mathbb{E}[Z_{22}]-\mathbb{E}[V]}{\sqrt{\var(V)}},

(288)

where (
287
) follows from
Z_{ij}
are identically distributed by the i.i.d. random codebook generation.

Now, assume that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\var(Z_{12})}{\var(V)}}

\displaystyle=\zeta,

(289)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}[Z_{12}]-\mathbb{E}[V]}{\sqrt{\var(V)}}

\displaystyle=\beta,

(290)

for some
\zeta,\beta\in\mathbb{R}

4
4

4

The existence of these limits can be proved easily.
. Then, by applying Slutsky’s theorem, from (

) and (

), we have

\displaystyle T\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}\zeta\min_{i\neq j}U_{ij}+\beta

(291)

and
U_{ij}

Since
\mathbb{E}[T]=0
and
\var(T)=1

\displaystyle T\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}\frac{\min_{i\neq j}U_{ij}-\mathbb{E}[\min_{i\neq j}U_{ij}]}{\sqrt{\var(\min_{i\neq j}U_{ij})}}.

(292)

Finally, from (

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{-\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{n}-\mathbb{E}[\frac{-\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{n}]}{\sqrt{\var{\big(\frac{-\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{n}}\big)}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}\frac{\min_{i\neq j}U_{ij}-\mathbb{E}[\min_{i\neq j}U_{ij}]}{\sqrt{\var(\min_{i\neq j}U_{ij})}}.

(293)

V-E
2
Constant-composition ensemble

In this part, we use Stein’s method to derive some criteria that provide sufficient conditions for the convergence in distribution to the normal random variable of the error probabilities and error exponents for general random coding ensemble over general channels, including the zero rate where
M_{n}\to\infty
as we mentioned. This includes other random codebooks than i.i.d. random codebook ensembles.

We start by showing that Theorem
5
also holds for the constant-composition codes.
In order to do this, we need some extra lemmas.

Lemma 14

Let
X
be a random variable on some finite set
\mathcal{X}
. Assume that for a certain
\beta>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[\{X\in\mathcal{A}_{1}\}\cap\{X\in\mathcal{A}_{2}\}\big]\leq\beta\mathbb{P}[X\in\mathcal{A}_{1}]\mathbb{P}[X\in\mathcal{A}_{2}]

(294)

holds for any
A_{1},A_{2}\subset\mathcal{X}
. Then, for any sequence of sets
\{A_{k}\}_{k=1}^{n-1}
such that
A_{k}\subset\mathcal{X}
and there exists
A_{i}\cap A_{j}=\emptyset
for some
i\neq j
and
i,j\in[n]
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigcap_{k=1}^{n}\big\{X\in\mathcal{A}_{k}\big\}\bigg]\leq\beta\prod_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\big[X\in\mathcal{A}_{k}\big].

(295)

Proof:

Appendix
A-L

∎

Next, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 15

Let
\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
be the set of all
n
-joint-types in
\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}
and let
Q_{XX^{\prime}},\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{N}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
such that
Q_{XX^{\prime}}\neq\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}
. Let
\{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2},\cdots,\bm{X}_{M}\}
be codewords of a constant-composition code with type
Q
. For a fixed pair
(i,j)
with
i\neq j
and
i,j\in[n]
, define
Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}
and
Z_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}=\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}})\}
. Then, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[\{Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=a\}\cap\{Z_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}=b\}\big]\leq\frac{1}{1-2^{-nI_{\min}}(Q)}\mathbb{P}\big[Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=a\big]\mathbb{P}\big[Z_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}=b\big]\bigg|,

(296)

for any
(a,b)\in\{0,1\}\times\{0,1\}
. Here,

\displaystyle I_{\min}(Q)=\min_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}):I_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}(X;X^{\prime})>0}I_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}(X;X^{\prime})>0.

(297)

Proof:

Appendix
A-M

∎

Corollary 1

Let
\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
be the set of all
n
-joint types in
\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}
. For a constant-composition code and
i\neq j
and
i,j\in[M]
, let

\displaystyle Z_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}=\mathbf{1}\big\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big\}

(298)

for all
Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
. Then, for any vector
\{z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}
such that there are at least two joint types
Q_{XX^{\prime}}
and
\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}
such that
\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}\neq Q_{XX^{\prime}}
and
z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=z_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}=1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigcap_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}\{Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}\bigg]\leq\frac{1}{1-2^{-nI_{\min}}(Q)}\prod_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}\mathbb{P}\big[Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\big]

(299)

Proof:

Since the vector
\{z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}
has at least two
n
-joint types
Q_{XX^{\prime}}
and
\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}
such that
\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}\neq Q_{XX^{\prime}}
and
z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=z_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}=1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigcap_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}\{Z_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}=z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[\{Z_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}=z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}\cap\{Z_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}=z_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}\}\bigg]

(300)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\{Z_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}=1\}\cap\{Z_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}=1\}\bigg]

(301)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\bigg\}\cap\bigg\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}})\bigg\}\bigg]

(302)

\displaystyle=0.

(303)

This concludes our proof of this corollary. ∎Now, we show the following fact which is based on Stein’s method.

Lemma 16

Let
f
be a bounded function with bounded first and second derivative. Let
X_{1},X_{2},\cdots,X_{n}
be zero-mean random variables on
\mathbb{R}
such that
\mathbb{E}|X_{k}^{4}|<\infty
for all
k\in[n]
.
In addition, assume there exists a function
f:\mathbb{Z}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that
f(n)\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
and a set
\mathcal{V}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
with cardinality
|\mathcal{V}|
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[X_{1}=x_{1},X_{2}=x_{2},\cdots,X_{n}=x_{n}]\leq\big(1+o(1)\big)\prod_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}[X_{k}=x_{k}]

(304)

for all
x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{n}\in\mathcal{V}
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[X_{1}=x_{1},X_{2}=x_{2},\cdots,X_{n}=x_{n}]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-n\zeta},

(305)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}|x_{k}|^{4}

\displaystyle\leq g(n),\quad\forall(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathcal{V}^{c}

(306)

for some
\zeta>0
.
Assume also that:

\displaystyle 2^{-n\zeta}|\mathcal{V}^{c}|g(n)\to 0\quad\mbox{as}\quad n\to\infty,

(307)

\displaystyle ng(n)\to\infty\qquad\mbox{as}\quad n\to\infty.

(308)

Let
S_{n}=X_{1}+X_{2}+\cdots+X_{n}
and

\displaystyle\tilde{T}=\frac{S_{n}}{\sqrt{\var(S_{n})}}.

(309)

Then, under the condition that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[|X_{i}^{3}|]\to 0,

(310)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{4}]\to 0,

(311)

we have

\displaystyle\tilde{T}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

(312)

Proof:

Now, we return to proof Theorem
5
. As in the i.i.d. case, we have

\displaystyle\frac{-\log P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{n}\xrightarrow{(\rm a.s.)}\frac{V}{n},

(313)

where

\displaystyle V=\min_{i\neq j}Z_{ij}

(314)

with

\displaystyle Z_{ij}=-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\},

(315)

for all
i,j\in[M]
and
i\neq j
.

For the constant-composition code, we have

\displaystyle Z_{ij}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}

(316)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}

(317)

\displaystyle=-n\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}.

(318)

Let

\displaystyle U_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{Z}_{ij}-\mathbb{E}[\bar{Z}_{ij}]}{\sqrt{\var(\bar{Z}_{ij})}}

(319)

where

\displaystyle\bar{Z}_{ij}=\frac{Z_{ij}}{n}=-\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}.

(320)

Then, we have

\displaystyle\frac{Z_{ij}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{ij}]}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{i}j)}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}U_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}},

(321)

and

\displaystyle U_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}(Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=\frac{-\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbb{P}\big(Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=1\big)}{\sqrt{\var(\bar{Z}_{ij})}}

(322)

where

\displaystyle Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}.

(323)

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}_{1}\cup\mathcal{V}_{2}\cup\mathcal{V}_{3},

(324)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{1}

\displaystyle=\bigg\{\{z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}:\mbox{there are at least two different $n$-joint types $Q_{XX^{\prime}}$ and $\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}$}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mbox{such that $z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=z_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}=1$}\bigg\},

(325)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2}=\bigg\{\{z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}:\mbox{there is exactly one $n$-joint types $Q_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}$}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mbox{such that $z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}^{*}=1$ and $Q_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}=Q_{X}^{*}Q_{X^{\prime}}^{*}$}\bigg\},

(326)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{3}=\bigg\{\{z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}:\mbox{such that $z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=1$ for all $Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})$}\bigg\}.

(327)

Now, for any
\{z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{V}}
, there are three subcases:

•

\{z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\in\mathcal{V}_{3}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigcap_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}\{Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigcap_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}\bigg]

(328)

\displaystyle=0

(329)

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}\mathbb{P}\big[Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\big].

(330)

•

\{z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\in\mathcal{V}_{2}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}}^{*})\big]

\displaystyle\doteq 1.

(331)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigcap_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}\{Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigcap_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}\bigg]

(332)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\{Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}}=1\}\cap\bigcap_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}):Q_{XX^{\prime}}\neq Q_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}}\{Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=0\}\bigg]

(333)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}}^{*})\}\cap\bigcap_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}):Q_{XX^{\prime}}\neq Q_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\notin\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}\bigg]

(334)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\bigg[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}}^{*})\bigg].

(335)

On the other hand, it is known that

\displaystyle\prod_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}):Q_{XX^{\prime}}\neq Q_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}}\mathbb{P}\big[Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\prod_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}):Q_{XX^{\prime}}\neq Q_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}}\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\notin\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\doteq\prod_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}):Q_{XX^{\prime}}\neq Q_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}}\big(1-2^{-nI_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}(X;X^{\prime})}\big)

(337)

\displaystyle\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle.}}{{\geq}}\prod_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}):Q_{XX^{\prime}}\neq Q_{XX^{\prime}}^{*}}\big(1-2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}\big)

(338)

\displaystyle\qquad=\big(1-2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}\big)^{|\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})|-1}

(339)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq 1-\big(|\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})|-1\big)2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}

(340)

\displaystyle\qquad=1+o(1),

(341)

where (
340
) follows from
(1-x)^{n}\geq 1-nx
for all

, and (

.

From (

) and (

), we have

(342)

•

\{z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\in\mathcal{V}_{1}
. Then, from Lemma

and Lemma

, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigcap_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}\{Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}\bigg]\leq(1+o(1))\prod_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}\mathbb{P}\big[Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\big].

(343)

From case 1, case 2, and case 3, on
\mathcal{V}

Now, for
\{z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\in\mathcal{V}^{c}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigcap_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})}\{Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\}\bigg]

(347)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\bigg[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}}^{*})\bigg]

(348)

\displaystyle\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle.}}{{\leq}}2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}.

(349)

In addition, on
\mathcal{V}^{c}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\frac{U_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}^{4}}{\var(\bar{Z}_{ij})}\leq\bigg(\var(\bar{Z}_{ij})\bigg)^{-1}\bigg(\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}U_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}^{4}\bigg).

(350)

Now, observe that

\displaystyle\var(\bar{Z}_{ij})=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}\bigg)^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad-\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}\bigg]\bigg)^{2}

(351)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}\sum_{y,y^{\prime}}\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}(y,y^{\prime})Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(y,y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}})\}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad-\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}\bigg]\bigg)^{2}

(352)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}\sum_{y,y^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(y,y^{\prime})Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(y,y^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad-\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}\bigg]\bigg)^{2}

(353)

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}\sum_{y,y^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(y,y^{\prime})Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(y,y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad-\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\}\bigg]\bigg)^{2}

(354)

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\bigg(\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}

(355)

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\big(\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\big)^{2}\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-2\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}\sum_{y,y^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}(y,y^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(y,y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}})\big].

(356)

Since
Q_{XX^{\prime}}\neq\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}})\big]

\displaystyle\doteq 2^{-nI_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}(X;X^{\prime})}2^{-nI_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}(X;X^{\prime})}

(357)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}.

(358)

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}\sum_{y,y^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}(y,y^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(y,y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}})\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}\sum_{y,y^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})\tilde{Q}_{XX^{\prime}}(y,y^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(y,y^{\prime})

(359)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}\bigg(\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}

(360)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}d_{\max}^{2}\bigg(\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}

(361)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}d_{\max}^{2}|\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})|^{2}

(362)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}d_{\max}^{2}(n+1)^{2|\mathcal{X}|},

(363)

where

\displaystyle d_{\max}=\max_{x,x^{\prime}}\bigg[-\log\bigg(\sum_{y}\sqrt{W(y|x)W(y|x^{\prime})}\bigg)\bigg].

(364)

From (
356
) and (
363
), we obtain

\displaystyle\var(\bar{Z}_{ij})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\big(\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\big)^{2}\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}-2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}d_{\max}^{2}(n+1)^{2|\mathcal{X}|}

(365)

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\bigg(1-\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}d_{\max}^{2}(n+1)^{2|\mathcal{X}|}

(366)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}:Q_{XX^{\prime}}\neq Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\bigg(1-\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}d_{\max}^{2}(n+1)^{2|\mathcal{X}|}

(367)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}:Q_{XX^{\prime}}\neq Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\bigg(1-2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}d_{\max}^{2}(n+1)^{2|\mathcal{X}|}

(368)

\displaystyle\geq\bigg(1-2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}\bigg)d_{\min}^{2}\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}:Q_{XX^{\prime}}\neq Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]-2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}d_{\max}^{2}(n+1)^{2|\mathcal{X}|}

(369)

where

\displaystyle d_{\min}=\min_{x,x^{\prime}}\bigg[-\log\bigg(\sum_{y}\sqrt{W(y|x)W(y|x^{\prime})}\bigg)\bigg]>0.

(370)

Now, observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}:Q_{XX^{\prime}}\neq Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=1-\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})\big]

(371)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=1-\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})}2^{-nH(Q_{X})}2^{-nH(Q_{X}^{\prime})}

(372)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=1-\big|\mathcal{T}(Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})\big|2^{-nH(Q_{X})}2^{-nH(Q_{X}^{\prime})}.

(373)

Now, by
[
29
]
, it holds that

\displaystyle 2^{nH(Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})}(n+1)^{-|\mathcal{X}|}\leq\big|\mathcal{T}(Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})\big|\leq 2^{nH(Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})}.

(374)

We can assume that
\big|\mathcal{T}(Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})\big|\neq 2^{nH(Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})}
, then

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{T}(Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})\big|\leq(\mbox{poly}(n))^{-1}2^{nH(Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}})}

(375)

where
\mbox{poly}(n)\neq 1
where
\mbox{poly}(n)
is some polynomial in
n
. Then, from (
373
) and
Q_{X}=Q_{X^{\prime}}=Q
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}:Q_{XX^{\prime}}\neq Q_{X}Q_{X^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}\big[(\bm{X}_{i},\bm{X}_{j})\in\mathcal{T}(Q_{XX^{\prime}})\big]\geq 1-(\mbox{poly}(n))^{-1}\geq 1-\alpha

(376)

for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
n
sufficiently large.

Hence, from (
369
), (
373
), and (
376
), we have

\displaystyle\var(\bar{Z}_{ij})

\displaystyle\geq\bigg(1-2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}\bigg)d_{\min}^{2}(1-\alpha)-2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}d_{\max}^{2}(n+1)^{2|\mathcal{X}|}

(377)

\displaystyle=\Omega(1).

(378)

From (
350
) and (
378
),

\displaystyle\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\frac{V^{4}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}(Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}})}{\var(\bar{Z}_{ij})}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\var(\bar{Z}_{ij})\bigg)^{-1}\bigg(\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}V_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}^{4}(Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}})\bigg)

(379)

\displaystyle\qquad=O(1)\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}V_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}^{4}(Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}})

(380)

where

\displaystyle V_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}(Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}+\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbb{P}\big(Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=1\big).

(381)

Note that

\displaystyle\big|V_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}(Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}})\big|^{4}

\displaystyle\leq 2\bigg(\bigg|\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\bigg|^{4}+\bigg|\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbb{P}\big(Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=1\big)\bigg|^{4}\bigg)

(382)

\displaystyle\leq 4d_{\max}^{4}

(383)

for all
Q_{XX^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})
, and

\displaystyle|V_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}(Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}})|^{3}

\displaystyle\leq 2\bigg(\bigg|\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\bigg|^{3}+\bigg|\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}Q_{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbb{P}\big(Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}=1\big)\bigg|^{4}\bigg)

(384)

\displaystyle\leq 4d_{\max}^{3}

(385)

Hence, from (
380
) and (
383
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\frac{V^{4}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}(Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}})}{\var(\bar{Z}_{ij})}

\displaystyle\leq 4|\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})|d_{\max}^{4}

(386)

\displaystyle\leq 4(n+1)^{|\mathcal{X}|}d_{\max}^{4}.

(387)

From (
349
) and (
387
), we have

\displaystyle 2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}|\mathcal{V}^{c}|\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}\frac{V^{4}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}(Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}})}{\var(\bar{Z}_{ij})}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-nI_{\min}(Q)}(n+1)^{|\mathcal{X}|}4(n+1)^{|\mathcal{X}|}d_{\max}^{4}\to 0

(388)

as
n\to\infty
.

Now, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})|^{2}}\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}V^{4}_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}(Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})|}4d_{\max}^{4}

(389)

\displaystyle\doteq\frac{1}{2^{nH(Q_{XX^{\prime}})}}4d_{\max}^{4}

(390)

\displaystyle\to 0

(391)

as
n\to\infty
, where (
389
) follows from (
386
).

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})|^{3/2}}\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}|V_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}(Z_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}})|^{3}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{T}_{n}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X})|^{3/2}}\sum_{Q_{XX^{\prime}}}4d_{\max}^{3}

(392)

\displaystyle\to 0.

(393)

From the above facts and Lemma
16
, we conclude that

\displaystyle T_{ij}=\frac{Z_{ij}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{ij}]}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{ij})}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

(394)

Similarly, we can prove that if
M
is a constant, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\neq j}\alpha_{ij}T_{ij}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

(395)

for any sequence
\{\alpha_{ij}\}_{i,j\in[M],i\neq j}
. Then, by using the same arguments as the proof of Lemma
5
for the rest, we obtain our result in (
23
).

V-F

Proof of Theorem
6

Our proof of this theorem is based on a modification of the Wasserstein metric, inspired by the classical Kolmogorov and Wasserstein metrics, that measures the distance between the distribution of the error exponent and that of the standard Gaussian. Such modification is needed to deal with an infinite number of terms as
n\to\infty
, a case where the classical Wasserstein metric upper bound fails to work
[
25
, Prop. 2.4]
.

Recall the definitions of probability metrics in Definition
2
. First, we prove the following fundamental lemma.

Lemma 17

If
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, then for any random variable
T
, it holds that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}(T\leq x)-\mathbb{P}(Z\leq x)\big|\leq 2(8\pi)^{-1/4}\sqrt{d_{W,\rm{mod}}(T,Z)}+\big|\mathbb{P}(T\leq x)-\mathbb{P}(T\geq-x)\big|

(396)

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. In addition, if the distribution of
T
is tight
5
5

5

A distribution on
(\mathbb{R},\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}))
is tight if for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
u,v\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\mathbb{P}(u<T\leq v)>1-\varepsilon

[
23
]
.
, for any
x\to 0
, which is a continuous point of the limit distribution of
T
, as
N\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{N\to\infty}\big|\mathbb{P}(T\leq x)-\mathbb{P}(Z\leq x)\big|\leq 2(8\pi)^{-1/4}\limsup_{N\to\infty}\sqrt{d_{W,\rm{mod}}(T,Z)}.

(397)

Proof:

Appendix
A-O
.
∎

By using the definition of
d_{W,\rm{mod}}
and setting
T=X
and
Y=Z
where
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, we obtain the following result, which is tighter than (or at least equal to) the upper bound of
d_{K}(T,Z)
in
[
25
, Prop. 2.4]
. However, we note that the probability metric here is the modified Wasserstein metric. See the same arguments to achieve a similar result in
[
25
, Prop. 2.4]
.

Lemma 18

For
h\in\mathcal{H}
, let
f_{h}
solve

\displaystyle f_{h}^{\prime}(w)-wf_{h}(w)=h(w)-\mathbb{E}[h(Z)].

(398)

If
T
is a random variable and
Z
has the standard normal distribution, then

\displaystyle d_{W,\rm{mod}}(T,Z)\leq\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\min\bigg\{\big|\mathbb{E}\big[f_{h}^{\prime}(T)-Tf_{h}(T)\big]\big|,\big|\mathbb{E}\big[f_{h}^{\prime}(-T)+Tf_{h}(-T)\big]\big|\bigg\}.

(399)

Proof:

Left as exercise.
∎Now, we prove the following lemma.

Lemma 19

Assume that
T=\min\{T_{1},T_{2},\cdots,T_{L}\}
for some
L\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
and
T_{1},T_{2},\cdots,T_{L}
are identically distributed random variables. Then, it holds that

\displaystyle d_{W,\rm{mod}}(T,Z)

\displaystyle\leq\max\bigg\{\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\big|\mathbb{E}[f_{h}^{\prime}(T_{1})-T_{1}f_{h}(T_{1})]\big|,\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\big|\mathbb{E}[f_{h}^{\prime}(-T_{1})+T_{1}f_{h}(-T_{1})]\big|\bigg\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\min\bigg\{\mathbb{E}[h(T)-h(T_{1})],\mathbb{E}[h(-T_{1})-h(-T)]\bigg\}.

(400)

Proof:

Appendix
A-P
.
∎

Lemma 20

[
25
, Th. 3.2]
Let
X_{1},X_{2},\cdots,X_{n}
be independent mean zero random variables such that
\mathbb{E}[|X_{i}|^{4}]<\infty
and
\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]=1
. If
T=\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}/\sqrt{n}
and
Z
has the standard normal distribution, then

\displaystyle\max\bigg\{\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[f^{\prime}_{h}(T)-Tf_{h}(T)\big]\big|,\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[f^{\prime}_{h}(-T)+Tf_{h}(-T)\big]\big|\bigg\}\leq\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[|X_{i}|^{3}]+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{n\sqrt{\pi}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{4}]}.

(401)

We can observe the fact (
401
) since
T
and
-T
are both the sums of independent random variables. Now, we are ready to prove Theorem
6
. Observe that

\displaystyle\max_{i\neq j}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j})\leq P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{M_{n}}\sum_{j\neq i}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j})\leq M_{n}(M_{n}-1)\max_{i\neq j}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j}).

(402)

Hence, for
M_{n}
sub-exponential in
n
, it holds that

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\xrightarrow{(\rm a.s.)}\frac{V_{n}}{n},

(403)

where

\displaystyle V_{n}=\min_{i\neq j}Z_{ij}(n)

(404)

with

\displaystyle Z_{ij}(n)=-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\},

(405)

for all
i,j\in[M_{n}]
and
i\neq j
(See the proof for this fact from the proof of Theorem
5
).

Define

\displaystyle T_{ij}(n)=\frac{Z_{ij}(n)-\mathbb{E}[Z_{ij}(n)]}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{ij}(n))}},

(406)

we have

\displaystyle\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)

\displaystyle=\min_{i\neq j}\frac{Z_{ij}(n)-\mathbb{E}[Z_{ij}(n)]}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{ij}(n))}}.

(407)

Now, for any
\varepsilon>0
, let the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{n}=\bigg\{\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\bigg|\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{Z_{ij}(n)-\mathbb{E}[Z_{ij}(n)]}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{ij}(n))}}\bigg|\geq\varepsilon\bigg\}

(408)

for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigg[\frac{1}{(M_{n}-1)M_{n}}\bigg|\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{Z_{ij}(n)-\mathbb{E}[Z_{ij}(n)]}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{ij}(n))}}\bigg|\geq\varepsilon\bigg]

(409)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}M_{n}^{2}(M_{n}-1)^{2}}\var\bigg(\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{Z_{ij}(n)-\mathbb{E}[Z_{ij}(n)]}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{ij}(n))}}\bigg)

(410)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}M_{n}^{2}(M_{n}-1)^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\var\bigg(\frac{Z_{ij}(n)-\mathbb{E}[Z_{ij}(n)]}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{ij}(N))}}\bigg)

(411)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}

(412)

\displaystyle<\infty,

(413)

where (
410
) follows from Chebyshev’s inequality, (
411
) follows from the pairwise independence of
Z_{ij}
’s, and (
413
) follows from the condition (
26
).

Hence, by Borel–Cantelli lemma, from (
413
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigcup_{n=1}^{\infty}\bigcap_{k=n}^{\infty}\mathcal{E}_{k}^{c}\bigg]=1.

(414)

However, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigcup_{n=1}^{\infty}\bigcap_{k=n}^{\infty}\mathcal{E}_{k}^{c}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigcup_{n=1}^{\infty}\bigcap_{k=n}^{\infty}\bigg\{\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\bigg|\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{Z_{ij}(k)-\mathbb{E}[Z_{ij}(k)]}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{ij}(k))}}\bigg|<\varepsilon\bigg\}\bigg].

(415)

It follows from (
414
) and (
415
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigcup_{n=1}^{\infty}\bigcap_{k=n}^{\infty}\bigg\{\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\bigg|\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{Z_{ij}(k)-\mathbb{E}[Z_{ij}(k)]}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{ij}(k))}}\bigg|<\varepsilon\bigg\}\bigg]=1,

(416)

or

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{Z_{ij}(n)-\mathbb{E}[Z_{12}(n)]}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{12}(n))}}\xrightarrow{(\rm a.s.)}0,

(417)

as
n\to\infty
. Hence, there exists a subset
\mathcal{A}
such that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A})=1
and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{Z_{ij}(n)-\mathbb{E}[Z_{12}(n)]}{\sqrt{\var(Z_{12}(n))}}\to 0

(418)

on
\mathcal{A}
.

Now, from Theorem
5
, we have
T_{ij}(n),T_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(n),T_{12}(n)
are independent as
n\to\infty
if
(i,j)\neq(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\neq(1,2)
. Then, for any
B_{1},B_{2}\in\mathcal{B}(R)
(Borel sets in
\mathbb{R}
), as
n\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg\{T_{ij}(n)-T_{12}(n)\in B_{1}\bigg\}\cap\bigg\{T_{ij}(n)-T_{12}(n)\in B_{2}\bigg\}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg\{T_{ij}(n)-T_{12}(n)\in B_{1}\bigg\}\cap\bigg\{T_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(n)-T_{12}(n)\in B_{2}\bigg\}\bigg|T_{12}(n)=\alpha\bigg]f_{T_{12}(n)}(\alpha)d\alpha

(419)

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg\{T_{ij}(n)\in\alpha+B_{1}\bigg\}\cap\bigg\{T_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(n)\in\alpha+B_{2}\bigg\}\bigg|T_{12}(n)=\alpha\bigg]f_{T_{12}(n)}(\alpha)d\alpha

(420)

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg\{T_{ij}(n)\in\alpha+B_{1}\bigg\}\cap\bigg\{T_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(n)\in\alpha+B_{2}\bigg\}\bigg]f_{T_{12}(n)}(\alpha)d\alpha

(421)

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\big[T_{ij}(n)\in\alpha+B_{1}\big]\mathbb{P}\big[T_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(n)\in\alpha+B_{2}\big]f_{T_{12}}(\alpha)d\alpha

(422)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\big[T_{ij}(n)-T_{12}(n)\in B_{1}\big]\mathbb{P}\big[T_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(n)-T_{12}(n)\in B_{2}\big]+o(1),

(423)

i.e.,
T_{ij}(n)-T_{12}(n)
and
T_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(n)-T_{12}(n)
are asymptotically independent. This means that
\{T_{ij}(n)-T_{12}(n)\}
are asymptotically pairwise independent. Hence, by using the same arguments to achieve (
417
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)-T_{12}(n)\xrightarrow{(\rm a.s.)}0,

(424)

as
n\to\infty
(point-wise convergence). Then, there exist a subset
\mathcal{B}
such that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B})=1
, and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)-T_{12}(n)\to 0

(425)

on
\mathcal{B}
as
n\to\infty
.

Hence, we have
P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})=1
, where
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{B}
. It follows that, for any
\varepsilon>0
, on the set
\mathcal{C}
, as
n
sufficiently large (which depends on each realization of
\{T_{ij}(n)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
’s), we have

\displaystyle-\varepsilon

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)<\varepsilon,

(426)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)-T_{12}(n)<\varepsilon.

(427)

The first step consists of showing that
\min\big\{\mathbb{E}[h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))],\mathbb{E}[h(-T_{12}(n))-h(-\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))]\big\}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. We carry out with two sub-steps, step 1a and step 1b.

V-F
1
Step 1a

To begin with, we prove that
\mathbb{E}[h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))]\to 0
as
n\to\infty
for all
h\in\{\mathcal{H}:a\geq 0\}
. We divide into different cases based on the value of
a
as following:

•

Case 1:
\liminf_{n\to\infty}a>0
.

Now, take an arbitrary small
\varepsilon>0
such that
\varepsilon<a
as
n\to\infty
. From (
426
), we have
\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)<\varepsilon<a
. It follows that

\displaystyle h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))=h\bigg(\frac{1}{M(M-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\bigg)=c

(428)

by the definition of
\mathcal{H}
.

Then, we have

\displaystyle h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))

\displaystyle=\bigg[h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h\bigg(\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\bigg[h\bigg(\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\bigg)-h(T_{12}(n))\bigg]

(429)

\displaystyle=h\bigg(\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\bigg)-h(T_{12}(n))

(430)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|h\bigg(\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\bigg)-h(T_{12}(n))\bigg|.

(431)

•

Case 2:
a\geq 0
and
\lim_{n\to\infty}a=0
.

Then, if
\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq a
as
n\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h\bigg(\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\bigg)

\displaystyle=h(a)-h\bigg(\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\bigg)

(432)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|a-\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\bigg|

(433)

\displaystyle\leq\max\bigg\{a,\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\bigg\}

(434)

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon,

(435)

where (
433
) follows from
1
-Lipschitz property of
h
for all
h\in\mathcal{V}
.

On the other hand, if
a<\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq\varepsilon
and
\liminf_{n\to\infty}c>0
, we have

\displaystyle h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h\bigg(\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)-\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)

(436)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon-a

(437)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon.

(438)

In addition, if
a<\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq\varepsilon
and
\lim_{n\to\infty}c=0
, we have

\displaystyle h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h\bigg(\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq c

(439)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon.

(440)

From (
435
), (
438
), and (
440
), it holds that

\displaystyle h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h\bigg(\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\bigg)\leq 2\varepsilon

(441)

for this case.

From (
431
) and (
441
), we have

\displaystyle h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon+\bigg|h\bigg(\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\bigg)-h(T_{12}(n))\bigg|

(442)

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon+\bigg|\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)-T_{12}(n)\bigg|

(443)

on
\mathcal{C}
.

From (
443
), on
\mathcal{C}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\big(h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))\big)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}\}]+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\big(h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij})-h(T_{12})\big)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}^{c}\}]

(444)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\big(h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))\big)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}\}]+c\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{C}^{c})

(445)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\big(h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))\big)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}\}].

(446)

Now, since
|\big(h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))\big)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}\}|\leq c
, hence by the bounded convergence theorem
[
23
]
, we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\lim_{n\to\infty}h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))\bigg]

(447)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(2\varepsilon+\bigg|\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)-T_{12}(n)\bigg|\bigg)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}\}\bigg]+c\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{C}^{c})

(448)

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon+\varepsilon

(449)

\displaystyle=3\varepsilon

(450)

for any
\varepsilon>0
, where (
449
) follows from (
427
).

From (

), by taking
\varepsilon\to 0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))\big]\leq 0.

(451)

Since
h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))\geq 0
, by the fact that
h
is non-increasing for all
h\in\mathcal{V}
, from (

), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))\big]=0.

(452)

Step 1b

Next, we prove that
\mathbb{E}[h(-\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(-T_{12}(n))]\to 0

\displaystyle h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))=\tilde{h}(T_{12}(n))-\tilde{h}(\min_{i\neq j}\{T_{ij}\}(n)).

(453)

Take an arbitrary
\varepsilon>0
, under the condition (
26
), from (

) and (

), as

\displaystyle-\varepsilon

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)<\varepsilon,,

(454)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{M(M-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)-T_{12}(n)<\varepsilon

(455)

on
\mathcal{C}
where

.

n\to\infty

Similar to Step 1a, we divide into different cases based on

\displaystyle<2\varepsilon.

(462)

In addition, if

\lim_{n\to\infty}c=0
. Hence,

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon+(a+c)

(464)

\displaystyle<2\varepsilon.

(465)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|-a-\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq-a-(a+c)

(468)

\displaystyle<2\varepsilon.

(469)

From (

), (

), and (

on
\mathcal{C}

), on

Now, since
|\big(\tilde{h}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-\tilde{h}(T_{12}(n))\big)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}\}|\leq c
, hence by the bounded convergence theorem
[

]
, we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\tilde{h}(T_{12}(n))-\tilde{h}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-\bigg]

(477)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(2\varepsilon+\bigg|\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)-T_{12}(n)\bigg|\bigg)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}\}\bigg]+c\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{C}^{c})

(478)

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon+\varepsilon

(479)

\displaystyle=3\varepsilon

(480)

for any
\varepsilon>0
, where (

).

From (

), by taking

(481)

for all

Step 2

In this step, we show that
\lim_{n\to\infty}d_{W,\rm{mod}}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij},Z)=0
. Indeed, from Lemma
20
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[f^{\prime}_{h}(T_{ij}(n))-T_{ij}(n)f_{h}(T_{ij}(n))\big]\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[|X_{k}|^{3}]+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{n\sqrt{\pi}}\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[X_{k}^{4}]}

(485)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}[|X_{1}|^{3}]+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{\pi n}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{4}]}

(486)

where

\displaystyle X_{k}:

\displaystyle=\frac{-\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\big(\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}-\mathbb{P}\big[(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\big]\big)}{\sqrt{\var\big(-\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\big)}},\qquad\forall k\in[N].

(487)

Now, observe that

\displaystyle\var(Z_{ij}(n))

\displaystyle=\var\bigg(-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\bigg)

(488)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\var\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\bigg)

(489)

\displaystyle=n\var\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\bigg),\quad\forall k\in[n],

(490)

where (
489
) and (
490
) follow from the fact that
(X_{ik},X_{jk})
are i.i.d. given
i,j
.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\var\bigg(-\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\bigg)^{2}\bigg]-\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\bigg]\bigg)^{2}

(491)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}^{2}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbb{P}\big[(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\big]-\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbb{P}\big[(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\big]\bigg)^{2}

(492)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}^{2}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})-\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})\bigg)^{2}=L_{2}.

(493)

In addition, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\big(\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}-\mathbb{P}\big[(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\big]\big)\bigg|^{3}\bigg]

(494)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 4\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}\bigg|^{3}\bigg]+\bigg|\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\mathbb{P}\big[(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\big]\bigg|^{3}\bigg)

(495)

\displaystyle\qquad=4\bigg[\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}^{3}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})+\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})\bigg)^{3}\bigg]=L_{3},

(496)

where (
495
) follows from
(a+b)^{3}\leq 4(|a|^{3}+|b^{3}|)
.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})\big(\mathbf{1}\{(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\}-\mathbb{P}\big[(X_{ik},X_{jk})=(x,x^{\prime})\big]\big)\bigg|^{4}\bigg]

(497)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 8\bigg[\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}^{4}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})+\bigg(\sum_{x,x^{\prime}}d_{\mathrm{B}}(x,x^{\prime})Q(x)Q(x^{\prime})\bigg)^{4}\bigg]=L_{4},

(498)

where we use
(a+b)^{4}\leq 8(a^{4}+b^{4})
in (
498
).

Hence, from (
486
), (

), (

), and (
498
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[f^{\prime}_{h}(T_{ij}(n))-T_{ij}(n)f_{h}(T_{ij}(n))\big]\big|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(\frac{L_{3}}{L_{2}^{3/2}}\bigg)+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi n}}\frac{L_{4}}{L_{2}^{2}},\qquad\forall i\neq j.

(499)

Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[f^{\prime}_{h}(-T_{ij}(n))+T_{ij}(n)f_{h}(-T_{ij}(n))\big]\big|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(\frac{L_{3}}{L_{2}^{3/2}}\bigg)+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi n}}\frac{L_{4}}{L_{2}^{2}},\qquad\forall i\neq j.

(500)

Since
T_{ij}(n)
’s (for
i\neq j
) are identically distributed by the random codebook generation, it follows from Lemma
19
and (
499
) that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle d_{W,\rm{mod}}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij},Z)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\max\bigg\{\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[f^{\prime}_{h}(T_{12}(n))-T_{12}(n)f_{h}(T_{12}(n))\big]\big|,\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[f^{\prime}_{h}(-T_{12}(n))+T_{12}(n)f_{h}(-T_{12}(n))\big]\big|\bigg\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\min\bigg\{\mathbb{E}[h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))],\mathbb{E}[h(-T_{12}(n))-h(-\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))]\bigg\}

(501)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(\frac{L_{3}}{L_{2}^{3/2}}\bigg)+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi n}}\frac{L_{4}}{L_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\min\bigg\{\mathbb{E}[h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))],\mathbb{E}[h(-T_{12}(n))-h(-\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))]\bigg\}\to 0,

(502)

where (
502
) follows from (
484
).

V-F
4
Step 3

In the third step, we whow that
\lim_{n\to\infty}|\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq x)-\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\geq-x)\big|=0
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
x
is a continuous point of the limiting distribution of
\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)
.

First, by the i.i.d. random codebook generation, observe that
T_{12}(n),T_{23}(n),\cdots,T_{(M-1)M}(n)
are i.i.d.. For any
x\in\mathbb{R}
, as
n,M_{n}\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\geq x\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big[\min\{T_{12}(n),T_{34}(n),\cdots,T_{(M-1)M}\}(n)\geq x\big]

(503)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}[T_{12}(n)\geq x]\mathbb{P}[T_{34}(n)\geq x]\cdots\mathbb{P}[T_{(M-1)M}(n)\geq x]

(504)

\displaystyle=\big(\mathbb{P}[T_{12}(n)\geq x]\bigg)^{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}

(505)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(Q(x)+o(1)\bigg)^{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}

(506)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{Q(\limsup_{N\to\infty}x)}{2}\bigg)^{\lfloor M/2\rfloor}

(507)

\displaystyle\to 0,

(508)

where (
506
) follows from the fact that
T_{12}(n)
is the sum of
n
i.i.d. terms, so we can apply the CLT, and (
507
) follows from the fact that as
n\to\infty
,
Q(x)\geq Q(\limsup_{n\to\infty}x)>Q(\limsup_{n\to\infty}x)/2
as
n
sufficiently large, and (
508
) follows from
M_{n}\to\infty
under the condition (
26
).

Now, we divide into different cases depending on
x
:

•

Case 1:
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
\liminf_{n\to\infty}x<0.

Then, there exists an
\varepsilon>0
such that
x<-\varepsilon
as
n
sufficiently large. Hence, under the condition (
26
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\geq-x)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\geq-x\bigg]

(509)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[\frac{1}{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\geq\varepsilon\bigg]

(510)

\displaystyle\to 0,

(511)

where (
511
) follows from (
417
).
It follows from (
508
) and (
511
) that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\big[\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\geq x\big]-\mathbb{P}[\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\geq-x]=0,

(512)

which leads to

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\big|\mathbb{P}\big[\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\geq x\big]-\mathbb{P}[\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\geq-x]\big|=0.

(513)

•

Case
\lim_{n\to\infty}x=0
.

From (
452
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[h(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h(T_{12}(n))\big]=0,

(514)

for all
h\in\mathcal{H}:\liminf_{n\to\infty}a\geq 0
.

Now, for any fixed constants
(u,v)
such that
0<u<v
, define
h_{u}(t)=\mathbf{1}\{t\leq x\}
and
h_{v}(t)=\mathbf{1}\{t\leq x\}
for all
t\in R
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\notin(u,v]\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\big[\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)>v\big]+\mathbb{P}\big[\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq u\big]

(515)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\bigg|\mathbb{P}\big[\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)>v\big]-\mathbb{P}(T_{12}(n)>v)\bigg]+\bigg[\mathbb{P}\big[\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq u\big]-\mathbb{P}(T_{12}(n)\leq u)\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{P}(T_{12}(n)>v)+\mathbb{P}(T_{12}(n)\leq u)

(516)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigg|\mathbb{P}\big[\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq v\big]-\mathbb{P}(T_{12}(n)\leq v)\bigg]+\bigg[\mathbb{P}\big[\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq u\big]-\mathbb{P}(T_{12}(n)\leq u)\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{P}(T_{12}(n)>v)+\mathbb{P}(T_{12}(n)\leq u)

(517)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigg|\mathbb{E}\big[h_{v}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h_{v}(T_{12}(n))\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[h_{u}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n))-h_{u}(T_{12}(n))\big]\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{P}(T_{12}(n)>v)+\mathbb{P}(T_{12}(n)\leq u)

(518)

\displaystyle\qquad=o(1)+\mathbb{P}(T_{12}(n)>v)+\mathbb{P}(T_{12}(n)\leq u)

(519)

\displaystyle\qquad=o(1)+Q(v)+Q(u),

(520)

where (
519
) follows from (
514
), and (
520
) follows from
T_{12}(n)\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
by the CLT (it is a sum of i.i.d. random variables with finite variance). Hence, for any
\varepsilon>0
, by letting
u
and
v
sufficiently large constants such that
Q(u)+Q(v)<\varepsilon/2
. Then, for
N
sufficiently large, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\in(u,v]\big)>1-\varepsilon.

(521)

This means that the probability distribution of
\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)
is tight. Then,
[
23
]
, the distribution of
\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)
is convergent to some distribution of a random variable
Y
. Hence, by Lemma
17
, we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}|\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq x)-\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\geq-x)\big|

\displaystyle\leq 2(8\pi)^{-1/4}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sqrt{d_{W,\rm{mod}}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n),Z)}=0,

(522)

where (
522
) follows from Step 3.

•

For any
x\geq 0
, then
-x\leq 0
. Hence, from two cases above, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\big|\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq-x)-\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\geq x)\big|=0.

(523)

However, for any continuous point
x
of the limiting distribution of
\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\big|\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq-x)-\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\geq x)\big|

(524)

\displaystyle\qquad=\lim_{n\to\infty}\big|(1-\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\geq-x))-(1-\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq x))\big|

(525)

\displaystyle\qquad=\lim_{n\to\infty}|\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq x)-\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\geq-x)\big|.

(526)

From (
523
) and (
526
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}|\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\leq x)-\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\neq j}T_{ij}(n)\geq-x)\big|=0.

(527)

V-G

Proof of Theorem
7

Consider first the case
0\leq R<C
. Since the random variable
P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})
takes values in
[0,1]
, we have that

\displaystyle\var[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})^{2}]-\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]^{2}

(534)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})^{2}]

(535)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\to 0

(536)

where (
536
) follows from the assumption that
\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\to 0
for
0\leq R<C
. Applying Chebyshev’s inequality we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl[\left|P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\right|\geq\delta\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\var[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\delta^{2}}

(537)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\delta^{2}}\to 0

(538)

where (
538
) follows from (
536
) and is valid for any given
\delta>0
.

Now let us consider the case
R>C
. The following hold:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\to 1

(539)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]^{2}\to 1

(540)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})^{2}]\geq\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]^{2}

(541)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})^{2}]\to 1

(542)

\displaystyle\var[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\to 0

(543)

where (
539
) follows from the theorem assumption, (
540
) follows from (
539
), (
541
) follows from Jensen’s inequality, (
542
) follows from (
540
) and (
541
) and the fact that
\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})^{2}]\leq 1
, while (
543
) follows from (
542
) and (
540
) and the additivity of limits.

Finally, using Chebyshev’s inequality again we find that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl[\left|P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\right|\geq\delta\Bigr]\leq\frac{\var[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\delta^{2}}\to 0

(544)

for any
\delta>0
.

V-H

Proof of Theorem
8

First, by the condition (
29
), we observe that

\displaystyle\frac{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}{\big(\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\big)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]-\big(\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\big)^{2}}{\big(\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\big)^{2}}

(545)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\big(\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\big)^{2}}-1

(546)

\displaystyle\to 0.

(547)

On the other hand, we know that

\frac{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}{\big(\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\big)^{2}}\to\infty
if
E_{\rm rce}(R)<E_{\rm trc}(R)
. Hence, from (
547
), we must have

\displaystyle E_{\rm trc}(R)=E_{\rm rce}(R).

(548)

Now, for any
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|-\frac{\log P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{N}-E_{\rm trc}(R)\bigg|>\varepsilon\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg\{P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}\bigg\}\cup\bigg\{P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)-\varepsilon)}\bigg\}\bigg]

(549)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}\bigg]+\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)-\varepsilon)}\bigg]

(550)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}\bigg]+2^{n(E_{\rm trc}(R)-\varepsilon)}\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]

(551)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}\bigg]+2^{n(E_{\rm trc}(R)-\varepsilon)}2^{-n(E_{\rm rce}(R)-\varepsilon/2\big)}

(552)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}\bigg]+2^{-n\varepsilon/2}

(553)

for
n
sufficiently large, where (
551
) follows from Markov’s inequality, and (
552
) follows from
\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\doteq 2^{-nE_{\rm rce}(R)}
, so
\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\leq 2^{-n(E_{\rm rce}(R)-\varepsilon/2)}
for
n
sufficiently large.

Now, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]<2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}-\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\bigg]

(554)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[-\big(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\big)>\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]-2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}\bigg].

(555)

Now, since
\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\doteq 2^{-nE_{\rm rce}(R)}=2^{-nE_{\rm trc}(R)}
by (
548
), so
\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]-2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}>0
for
n
sufficiently large. It follows from (
555
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[\big|P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\big|>\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]-2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}\bigg]

(556)

\displaystyle\approx\frac{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}{\big(\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\big)^{2}}

(557)

\displaystyle\to 0,

(558)

where (
557
) follows Markov’s inequality and the fact that
\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]-2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}=\Theta\big(\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\big)
, and (
558
) follows from (
547
).

From (
553
) and (
558
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-E_{\rm trc}(R)\bigg|>\varepsilon\bigg]\to 0,

(559)

or equivalently,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm p})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}E_{\rm trc}(R).

(560)

V-I

Proof of Theorem
9

From
[
15
, Th. 1]
and from (
111
), for
n
sufficiently large we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq\gamma_{n}^{\rho}\min_{\rho\in[1,\infty)}E[P_{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})^{\frac{1}{\rho}}]^{\rho}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq 2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)-\epsilon_{n})}\right]

(561)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{n}}

(562)

where
\gamma_{n}\to\infty
,
\frac{\log\gamma_{n}}{n}\to 0
and
\epsilon_{n}\to 0
.
On the other hand, the Paley-Zygmund inequality
[
26
]
implies that, for large enough
n
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq\delta_{n}\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq 2^{-n(E_{\rm rce}(R)+\epsilon^{\prime}_{n})}\right]

(563)

\displaystyle\geq(1-\delta_{n})^{2}\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]^{2}}{E[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})^{2}]}

(564)

where we choose a sequence
\delta_{n}
that goes to zero subexponentially, i.e.,
\epsilon^{\prime}_{n}\to 0
and
0<\delta_{n}<1

\forall n
.
Let
n_{0}
be such that
\Delta E>\epsilon_{n}
,
\forall n>n_{0}
. Note that such an
n_{0}
must exist from the definition of limit for
\epsilon_{n}
. Now consider the following chain of inequalities for a large enough
n
,
n>n_{0}
:

\displaystyle 2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)-\epsilon_{n})}

\displaystyle=2^{-n(E_{\rm rce}(R)+\Delta E-\epsilon_{n})}

(565)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-n(E_{\rm rce}(R)+\Delta E-\epsilon_{n_{0}})}

(566)

\displaystyle<2^{-n(E_{\rm rce}(R)+\epsilon^{\prime}_{n})}

(567)

where (
565
) is from the theorem statement, (
566
) is valid from a certain
n
onwards from the definition of limit for
\epsilon_{n}
, while (
567
) is because
\Delta E-\epsilon_{n_{0}}
is a positive constant and, for large enough
n
,
\epsilon^{\prime}_{n}<\Delta E-\epsilon_{n_{0}}
.
Now, using (
567
), (
561
) and (
563
) we have:

\displaystyle(1-\delta_{n})\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})^{2}]}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq\delta_{n}\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\right]

(568)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq 2^{-n(E_{\rm rce}(R)+\epsilon^{\prime}_{n})}\right]

(569)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq 2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)-\epsilon_{n})}\right]

(570)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\geq\gamma_{n}^{\rho}\min_{\rho\in[1,\infty)}\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})^{\frac{1}{\rho}}]^{\rho}\right]

(571)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{n}}

(572)

where(
570
) follows from (
567
). Finally, notice that, by definition, the following inequalities hold:

(1-\delta_{n})\to 1

\frac{1}{\gamma_{n}}\to 0

that imply:

\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})^{2}]}\to 0.

Under the condition

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var{P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}}}\to 0.

\displaystyle\frac{P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\rm e}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\longrightarrow\,}}}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

V-K

Proof of Theorem
11

First, if
\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\big(\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\big)^{2}}>1
, then it holds that

\displaystyle\nu=\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}<\infty.

(576)

Then, for
n
sufficiently large, we have

\displaystyle\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}-\nu

(577)

\displaystyle\geq-\nu,

(578)

hence

\displaystyle\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle({\rm d})}}{{\,\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.875pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}\,}}}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

(579)

Hence, the condition (
33
) implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\big(\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\big)^{2}}\to 1,

(580)

which leads to

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}\to\infty.

(581)

Now, for any
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-E_{\rm trc}(R)\bigg|>\varepsilon\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg\{P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}\bigg\}\cup\bigg\{P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)-\varepsilon)}\bigg\}\bigg]

(582)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}\bigg]+\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)-\varepsilon)}\bigg].

(583)

Furthermore, under the condition
\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}\to\infty
, we must have

\displaystyle E_{\rm trc}(R)=E_{\rm rce}(R).

(584)

Observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})>2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)-\varepsilon)}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{n(E_{\rm trc}(R)-\varepsilon)}\mathbb{E}\big[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\big]

(585)

\displaystyle\doteq 2^{n(E_{\rm trc}(R)-\varepsilon)}2^{-nE_{\rm rce}(R)}

(586)

\displaystyle=2^{-n\varepsilon}.

(587)

On the other hand, from (
33
), for
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})<2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}\leq\frac{2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}-\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}\bigg]

(588)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[Z\leq\frac{2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}-\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}\bigg]+o_{n}(1),

(589)

where (
589
) follows from the condition (
33
).

Now, since
2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}-\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]<0
for
n
sufficiently large since
\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]\doteq 2^{-nE_{\rm rce}(R)}
and
E_{\rm trc}(R)\geq E_{\rm rce}(R)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[Z\leq\frac{2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}-\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}\bigg]=Q\bigg(\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]-2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}\bigg)\to 0

(590)

as
n\to\infty
since

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]-2^{-n(E_{\rm trc}(R)+\varepsilon)}}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}))}}\to\infty.

(591)

From (
583
), (
587
), and (
591
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\bigg|-\frac{1}{n}\log P_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-E_{\rm trc}(R)\bigg|>\varepsilon\bigg]\to 0

(592)

or (
34
) holds.

V-L

Proof of Theorem
12

We first introduce a result which is developed in
[
25
]
for sum of random variables with local dependence.

Definition 3

We say that a collection of random variables
(Y_{1},Y_{2},\cdots,Y_{n})
has dependency neighbourhoods
\mathcal{N}_{i}\subset\{1,2,\cdots,n\},i=1,2,\cdots,n
, if
i\in\mathcal{N}_{i}
and
Y_{i}
is independent of
\{Y_{j}\}_{j\notin\mathcal{N}_{i}}
.

Lemma 21

[
25
, Th. 3.6]
Let
Y_{1},Y_{2},\cdots,Y_{n}
be random variables such that
\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}^{4}]<\infty
,
\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}=0]
,
\sigma^{2}=\var(\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i})
, and define
T=\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}/\sigma
. Let the collection
(Y_{1},Y_{2},\cdots,Y_{n})
have dependency neighborhoods
\mathcal{N}_{i},i=1,2,\cdots,n
, and also define
D=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|\mathcal{N}_{i}|
. Then for
Z
a standard normal random variable,

\displaystyle d_{W}(T,Z)\leq\frac{D^{2}}{\sigma^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}|Y_{i}|^{3}+\frac{\sqrt{28}D^{3/2}}{\sqrt{\pi}\sigma^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}^{4}]}.

(593)

Observe that

\displaystyle P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{M_{n}}\sum_{j\neq i}Y_{ij}

(594)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{M_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{M_{n}}\sum_{i<j\leq M_{n}}Y_{ij},

(595)

where

\displaystyle Y_{ij}=\mathbb{P}\big[\{\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j}\}\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{P}\big[\{\bm{X}_{i}\to\bm{X}_{j}\}\big]\big],\qquad\forall i,j\in[M_{n}]\times[M_{n}].

(596)

For i.i.d. random coding ensembles,
\{Y_{ij}\}_{1\leq i<j\leq M_{n}}
are pairwise independent and identically distributed by the symmetry of the random codebook ensemble. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=\var\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{M_{n}}\sum_{j\neq i}Y_{ij}\bigg)

(597)

\displaystyle=4\var\bigg(\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq M_{n}}Y_{ij}\bigg)

(598)

\displaystyle=4\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq M_{n}}\var(Y_{ij})

(599)

\displaystyle=2M_{n}(M_{n}-1)\gamma^{2}.

(600)

In addition, it is easy to see that
D\leq 2(M_{n}-1)
. Hence, by Lemma
21
, we have

\displaystyle d_{W}\bigg(\frac{P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})]}{\sqrt{\var(P_{\mathrm{e}}^{\rm ub}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}},Z\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4(M_{n}-1)^{2}}{(2M_{n}(M_{n}-1)\gamma^{2})^{3/2}}\bigg(\frac{M_{n}(M_{n}-1)}{2}\bigg)\mathbb{E}\big[|Y_{12}|^{3}\big]
